# C++20 Rewrite Status

[한국어](REWRITTEN.md)

Last updated: 2026-11-04

This document records progress and verification for the C++20 port of the entire cowsql/raft library. Follow [AGENTS.en.md](AGENTS.en.md) for work rules. Update this document and its Korean counterpart together. Write API documentation under the repository-root `docs/ko/` and `docs/en/` directories; do not copy development progress records into API documentation.

## Overall Status

RocksDB/SQLite storage modules (2026-11-04): pinned SDKs and required Tcl generation tools under deps and implemented all of `storage`, composable through `make_io` without UV. Verified eight related checks in static/shared Release and ASan/UBSan, forced-exit recovery/SQLite rollback, installed consumers, and disabling options. Also fixed existing duplicate node snapshots exposed by inline completion. DB calls block/completions run inline; scope, initial failures, and limits are recorded below.

Earlier user-selected I/O composition unit (2026-11-04): implemented `storage`/`timer`/`make_io`, UV storage/timer, and Asio timer. Users combine transport and an optional snapshot codec. Verified seven core contracts, a UV-free Asio TCP cluster, real per-codec snapshot restart, thirteen ASan/UBSan checks by scope, and static/shared installed consumers. Existing `uv_io` remains; UV is the only built-in durable storage provider. Scope, initial failures, and reuse conditions are recorded in the unit below.

Module API documentation unit (2026-11-04): added ten paired pages for core/UV, seven optional adapters, and a module index. Documented public APIs, ownership, callbacks, errors, options, examples, and navigation to existing topic pages. Checked thirty-one total document pairs/638 relative links and matching new code/signature blocks. Documentation-only changes skip product builds/tests and reuse existing results.

Added concrete adapters for core abstractions: common `byte_stream`, `executor`, `compression_codec`, `framed_transport`, and the seven modules' stream/executor/codec implementations. Eight new contract checks passed individually in Release and ASan/UBSan, with eighteen related UV/compression checks and static/shared installed consumers validated. The new unit below preserves initial failures/interruption and selective resumption. These modules do not provide QUIC connection/TLS establishment or a complete separate durable `io`; those boundaries remain with the application connector and existing UV.

User scope correction (2026-11-03): SDK include/link targets alone do not implement a module. Work has started on common byte-stream, executor and compression interfaces plus native adapters implementing core abstractions like UV. Earlier SDK smoke checks remain dependency preparation/link evidence only, not adapter behavior validation.

Started license consolidation and repository/build integration for seven optional dependency modules (`asyncpp`, `asio`, `mvfst`, `msquic`, `lz4`, `zstd`, `zlib`). Preserve the callback-based API and distinguish external SDK integration from actual Raft backend implementations.

Completed core/UV restructuring, active public-header generation, and split library/installation integration. Core/shared-native/minimal profiles, module-only consumers, and final static checks are validated.

Cleaned up paired API documents and root instructions/records around independent C++ library usage. Documentation-only changes; reused existing implementation/build/test results.

Added top-level umbrella header `include/raftpp.hpp` and connected installation/paired API documents. Header checks with optional features disabled and static/shared installed consumers passed. See its unit below for executed/skipped scope.

C-library removal, independent C++ test conversion, and formatting are complete. C++-only static/shared and optional-feature builds, selected 92 core/32 native checks, and the default development build passed. The project builds only C++ and retains the original license separately. See the cleanup unit below for executed/skipped/historical results and limits.

Previous functional-port completion audit, before this cleanup — finished the final requirements audit on 2026-11-03. Mapped all 51 original sources/ten feature groups, 114 public functions, and functional structure/I-O/FSM/raw-transport contracts to independent C++20 implementations and scoped verification evidence. No unported layer remains in consensus/storage/networking/public callback APIs/fixtures/optional features/installation/examples-tools. The final decision table below distinguishes implementation satisfaction from verification conditions.

That earlier audit found no missing exports among 279 non-inline declarations in the shared library (228 in the separate fixture-OFF profile), and no dependency on any of 371 original-C global symbols in three static/shared C++ artifacts. The 104 final source/input hashes are unchanged, so existing runtime/option/consumer results were reused. Filled only the paired API documentation gap for common ownership aliases; product/test/build rules are unchanged.

Verification limits: completion claims exclude actual kernel perf/trace collection/raw-device runs/all filesystem-permission combinations/32-bit execution/LSan. Preserve comparisons limited by undefined behavior/early frees in original C, the additional all-follower bounded-convergence condition that failed in both implementations, and the undetermined initial TCP-timeout record. The earlier default configuration registered 206 CTest checks; the C++-only configuration now registers 204. Neither count is a fresh whole-suite pass count. Outstanding work in earlier unit summaries describes those units at the time; this section and the final audit table govern current status.

Previous tracing unit: implemented independent tracing APIs and basic consensus/node/file/TCP diagnostics. Connected standard source locations, shared callable lifetimes, levels, no-op/stderr/environment behavior, and writer metrics. Fifty affected Debug checks, six new-feature shared Release checks, and seventeen ASan/UBSan checks passed. Reused three earlier tracing-unit passes from this same work. Skipped unchanged format/storage-value checks and the full option matrix. API/verification scopes are recorded below; the full port remains in progress.

Previous TCP unit: independently implemented concrete `tcp_transport` and connected it to public `uv_io`. Ported C handshakes, IPv4 addresses, six RPC streams, send queues, retries, and asynchronous shutdown. Ten new TCP checks passed by scope in Debug, strict shared Release, and ASan/UBSan. An actual disk-and-TCP three-node cluster verified elections, commands, leadership transfer, snapshot catch-up, and full restart. Skipped the existing sixty-six tests and reused their results because their executed paths did not change. The full library port remains in progress.

Previous public I/O unit: implemented snapshot preparation retries, barrier-ordered publication/retention, retrieval, and close in a native file coordinator. Added public `uv_io` and the `transport` boundary. Ten new tests passed, including native C file comparisons, lifetime/failure/allocation checks, actual-disk node restart, and a three-node partition/snapshot-catch-up sequence using actual disk storage and test message transport. All sixty-six default and ASan/UBSan tests and three external-consumer configurations passed; leak checking was excluded due to environment restrictions. Option execution/reuse scopes are recorded in the baseline table below; the redundant full rerun was interrupted at the user’s request. TCP sockets remain outstanding.

Earlier log-writing unit: capability probing, aligned batch staging, a two-file prepare pool, append/finalization, blocking/nonblocking barriers, and asynchronous truncation coordination are implemented and verified. Closed-segment names/bytes and capability results matched the actual native C backend. All fifty-six default and ASan/UBSan tests, six feature combinations (fifty-six tests with UV, thirty-four without), and three external-consumer configurations passed. Leak checks were excluded due to environment restrictions. Upper snapshot coordination, the full public I/O backend, networking, tracing, fixtures, and other layers remain outstanding.

Earlier writer unit: direct-I/O, KAIO, and threadpool asynchronous writing and request/buffer lifetime management are implemented and verified. All forty-eight default and ASan/UBSan tests, six feature combinations (forty-eight tests with UV, thirty-four without), and shared core-only, shared UV/LZ4, and static UV/LZ4 external consumers passed. Leak checking was excluded due to environment restrictions. After the final cumulative-length/offset checks, all forty-eight default tests and the five strict Release and ASan/UBSan writer tests passed again. Prepare pools, append/barrier coordination, filesystem capability probing, and network integration were outstanding at that point.

Earlier segment unit: synchronous segment-file creation, reading, open-file recovery, truncation, retention, and full stored-state loading are implemented and verified. All forty-three default and ASan/UBSan tests passed, as did six feature combinations (forty-three tests with UV, thirty-four without) and external shared core-only, shared UV/LZ4, and static UV/LZ4 consumers. Leak checking was excluded due to environment restrictions. Asynchronous append, prepare pools, barriers, and direct I/O are next.

Allocation-failure injection exposed termination inside libstdc++ directory_iterator, so directory scanning uses an RAII request from the existing libuv dependency. Paths, strings, containers, and sorting remain standard implementations.

Foundational components, the in-memory log, and snapshots are connected to the internal event-driven consensus core. In-memory cluster elections, replication, commits, membership, leadership transfer, partitions, and recovery are compared against C. Public callback node APIs and I/O and FSM interfaces are implemented and tested with a mock backend. RPC and disk-batch serialization and checksums are independently implemented with format comparisons and verification complete. Synchronous metadata, snapshot, and segment-file operations, stored-state recovery, a direct-I/O/KAIO writer, and a log-writing coordinator are also implemented. A test-only I/O adapter checks a single node's close/restart using actual disk storage. The snapshot/file coordinator and public `uv_io` now connect actual storage and timers to an abstract message transport. Concrete `tcp_transport` is connected to `uv_io` and nodes, allowing actual disk-and-TCP nodes. Basic tracing and buffer/object/core/native-container memory resources are connected. An independent public C++ virtual-time fixture provides memory I/O and cluster controls. Native examples, submit/disk benchmarks and kernel perf/trace are implemented. Current whole-API/compatibility decisions follow the final audit table below; actual kernel-measurement/device-execution limits remain separate.

The original C implementation is preserved under `raft/` as the behavioral reference. `raftpp::raft` provides the original C library; `raftpp::raftpp` provides independently implemented C++ components. A successful C-target build or an enabled feature flag does not establish that the corresponding C++ layer is complete.

### RocksDB/SQLite Durable Storage Modules (2026-11-04)

Added pinned `deps/rocksdb`, `deps/sqlite`, and `deps/tcl` submodules. RocksDB v11.13.2 is `17b624b5c0d41bd42db5cf88aa867f3b26eaf1fa`, SQLite 3.54.1 `57520227c95396ce39ba6ecaac8d475b78dd3964`, and Tcl 8.6.17 `433df5b20a8bd6957c3bda981c83d65202eb7de7`. Gitlinks/`deps/revisions.json`/checkouts match and their worktrees are clean. Existing SDK pins remain unchanged. Tcl generates SQLite's amalgamation from canonical sources and is a build tool, not a runtime dependency. Platform thread support is the required external runtime boundary for the two DBs.

Connected `RAFTPP_MODULE_ROCKSDB`/`RAFTPP_MODULE_SQLITE` (default OFF), `raftpp::rocksdb`/`raftpp::sqlite`, public headers/exports/features/umbrella/selective installation/consumers. `rocksdb_storage`/`sqlite_storage` implement all of core `storage`, beyond declarations/link smoke. Users pass them to `make_io` with timers/transports/optional snapshot codecs; no UV dependency is required. Core adds `storage_limits` and a common private KV storage algorithm without SDK includes, independently used by each module. Existing consensus algorithms/RPC/UV file formats remain intact; API documents specify the new DB-specific v1 keyspace/metadata/log/snapshot encoding. No automatic migration of UV file directories is provided.

Implemented term/vote/bootstrap/recover, log append/truncate, snapshot publication/installation/trailing retention, and restart load/get. RocksDB uses synchronous WAL-enabled `WriteBatch`; SQLite uses FULL synchronization, DELETE journaling, EXCLUSIVE connections/transactions, and rollback. Republishing the same snapshot index/term preserves later log entries; conflicting terms at that index are rejected. Cached state changes only after atomic commits. Checks cover size/count/index/format/length/log continuity/output preservation. DB calls/commits block the calling thread and successful completions are inline. A single serialized execution domain retains callbacks/request data and supports facade destruction/close within callbacks before native resource release. If physical write/synchronization failure leaves durability uncertain, close/reopen before subsequent operations.

New DB node checks exposed an existing driver issue: inline storage of the startup-configuration snapshot could submit its index again before its completion event reached core. Both DBs failed `node leader` with core `invalid` shutdown. Independently reproduced without DBs by setting threshold 1/trailing 0 in memory-backed `composed_io_node`. `node.cpp` now records successfully stored local snapshot indices and suppresses duplicate submission while the event waits. Immediate submission failure does not advance this index, permitting retries. Consensus state transitions themselves are unchanged.

Reference scope covers storage/io/composed_io/node inputs/outputs, metadata/log/snapshot ordering, failure/callback/cleanup, existing configuration/encoding/byte/buffer/error/memory models, actual SDK DB/iterator/write-batch/statement/transaction APIs and original licenses, and preparation/module/generated-header/install/consumer rules. Actual edits affect new SDKs/storage modules/common private algorithm/core limits/inline snapshot guard, related tests/builds, and paired API/progress documents. `docs/{ko,en}/modules/{rocksdb,sqlite}.md` and composition/dependencies/node/CMake/index documents contain usage contracts only.

Environment: Linux x86-64, GCC 13.3.0, CMake 3.28.3, Ninja. SDK preparation uses `/tmp/raftpp-database-sdk` and its `prefix` directory. RocksDB/SQLite SDKs are static PIC; Tcl is used only for source generation. Disabled RocksDB tests/examples/tools/benchmarks/gflags/liburing/optional compression and SQLite CLI/Tcl bindings/readline/extension loading/optional extensions/math functions. SQLite configure's zlib detection serves upstream shell code; verified no zlib/Tcl references in the DB archive built. No upstream whole suites were run. All adapter/installed-consumer validation uses Release.

### User-Selected I/O Composition (2026-11-04)

Added core `storage`, `timer`, `io_components`, and `make_io()`. Users supply required storage/timer/transport and an optional `compression_codec`, then use the existing request/completion-callback `io`/`node` API. No SDK combination or coroutine/future API is imposed. Core builds without optional SDKs. `executor` remains a callback-submission boundary and is not automatically converted into storage or a timer.

UV now provides `uv_storage`, reusing existing disk coordination, and a separate `uv_timer`; Asio provides `asio_timer`. The private storage-only path creates neither a network nor the periodic node timer, preserving existing file synchronization/retry/barrier/cleanup policies. Public `uv_io` construction/behavior remains intact. UV remains the only built-in durable storage provider. UV-free persistence requires user-provided `storage`; this unit does not claim file backends for Asio/QUIC adapters.

The composer exclusively manages component init/start/close lifecycles and retains accepted requests/batches/messages/original and encoded snapshots until completion. It handles synchronous/inline completion, submission failure, exceptions/OOM, callback reentrancy, facade destruction, and partial initialization failure. Close completes after every component/request/active callback drains and retained payloads are released. Components and node/FSM calls must share one serialized execution domain; no automatic thread/loop bridge is provided.

An optional codec stores only snapshot payloads in a v1 envelope (eight-byte magic, eight-byte little-endian encoding and original length) and decodes reads. Ordinary uncompressed payloads remain unchanged; new raw payloads colliding with reserved magic are escaped. Checks cover original/encoded limits, truncation/unsupported encodings/length mismatch/missing codecs, and output preservation on failure. RPC/log/metadata formats and existing `uv_io` raw/LZ4 paths remain unchanged. The new envelope is an opt-in storage path and stores no codec identifier/dictionary. Reopen through `make_io` with the same codec settings; legacy `uv_io` does not decode it. Preexisting raw payloads beginning with reserved magic require separate conversion.

Reference scope covers core I/O/node/FSM/transport/framing/snapshot/codec/buffer/error/random inputs/outputs/ownership/cleanup, UV file coordination/load/close/native timers, Asio executor/stream/timer and the SDK APIs used, module/generated-header/installation/example/test rules. Actual edits affect composition APIs/implementation, the UV storage facade/two timers, related builds/tests/consumers/examples, and paired API/progress documents. Consensus algorithms and existing disk layers were not rewritten. `docs/{ko,en}/composition.md` and module documents describe usage contracts only. New `raftpp-example-composed DIRECTORY [none|lz4|zstd|zlib] [uv|asio]` demonstrates snapshot publication/close and restart restoration with user-selected codecs/timers.

Environment/reuse conditions: Linux x86-64, GCC 13.3.0, CMake 3.28.3, libuv 1.48.0, existing SDK prefix `/tmp/raftpp-sdk-build/prefix` and upstream checkouts. All adapter/installed-consumer/example validation uses Release. Normal profiles use `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`; fixture/backtrace are OFF. Runs/skips are distinguished per profile below; counts are not whole-suite pass totals.

### Concrete Adapters for Core Abstractions (2026-11-03–04)

This unit adds actual implementations following the preceding SDK preparation/include/link stage. Core now provides `byte_stream`, `executor`, `compression_codec`, and `framed_transport`. UV streams implement the common byte stream. `asyncpp_executor` schedules callbacks on a dispatcher while retaining its lifetime; `asio_executor` and `asio_stream` implement native post and connected TCP sockets. `mvfst_stream` and `msquic_stream` adopt established bidirectional QUIC streams. LZ4/Zstandard/zlib modules implement actual codecs using upstream frame APIs. All seven modules are source libraries depending on core, with generated export headers, public headers, umbrella integration, and installation targets.

`framed_transport` uses the existing six RPC formats over registered peer streams, handling fragmented/coalesced input and request/message lifetimes. Stream adapters retain state through read/write/close completion and defer close completion while user callbacks execute. Asio read stop does not cancel writes and preserves already consumed bytes until resume. MsQuic retains buffers through SEND_COMPLETE and releases native streams after shutdown events. An OOM shutdown submission uses inline abort on the native worker. Codecs check bounded sizes, single frames, truncation/trailing data, input/output aliasing, output allocators, and output preservation on failure.

Reference/edit scope covers the new core boundaries and serialization/transport dependencies, native APIs actually called by each adapter and their ownership/callback/cleanup paths, module CMake and feature/installation generation, related tests/consumers, and API documents. Consensus/persistence algorithms and disk/network formats are unchanged. Prepared required SDKs including Folly/Fizz/mvfst; added `FindSodium.cmake` for installed mvfst dependency discovery. The Folly preparation profile excludes unused Linux AIO/SimpleAsyncIO objects, removing unnecessary unresolved host libaio symbols. Disabled unused glog unwinding with `WITH_UNWIND=none`. Upstream sources and complete suites were not modified/validated.

Environment: Linux x86-64, GCC 13.3.0, CMake 3.28.3, SDK prefix `/tmp/raftpp-sdk-build/prefix`. All executions use Release. The shared baseline uses `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`, all seven SDKs ON, UV/LZ4 ON, fixture/backtrace/examples/benchmarks OFF. Core-only is static with all SDKs/UV/LZ4/fixture/backtrace OFF and PkgConfig discovery disabled. The all-SDK static profile has UV/LZ4/fixture/backtrace OFF. ASan/UBSan uses shared libraries, all seven SDKs ON, UV/LZ4/fixture/backtrace OFF, `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-omit-frame-pointer`, `ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0`, and `UBSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1`. External SDK binaries are not sanitizer-instrumented.

### License Consolidation and Optional SDK Modules (2026-11-03)

Recorded the independent full C++20 implementation's direct derivative relationship to cowsql/raft in `LICENSE.md` and its English counterpart. Included upstream copyright/contribution notices, the LGPL 3.0 linking exception, and LGPL text verbatim, together with GPL 3.0 text. Removed root `licenses/`; attribution comments, installation, and current links reference the new license file. External SDKs retain their own licenses and original notices. Historical references to the old license path remain records of their original work units.

Initialized a root Git repository (main) and added the seven requested SDKs as pinned submodules in `deps/`. Added public-header/privates/src/CMake boundaries in `modules/{asyncpp,asio,mvfst,msquic,lz4,zstd,zlib}`. These are SDK integration interfaces exposing required upstream APIs, not completed Raft `io`/transport implementations or coroutine methods. Existing core io/message_transport abstract contracts are SDK-independent, so consensus, memory, and shutdown contracts were not rewritten.

Core is always mandatory; all optional modules depend on `raftpp::core`. Only SDK preparation is a separate CMake project. Default-OFF `RAFTPP_MODULE_<NAME>` selects SDK modules; `RAFTPP_DEPENDENCIES` selects SDK preparation. Connected active-header generation, the top-level umbrella, installation, and package dependencies, removing disabled SDK header directories too. `RAFTPP_MODULE_LZ4` is independent of UV and distinct from existing UV decoder option `RAFTPP_WITH_LZ4`. With both enabled UV uses the LZ4 module. Selecting zstd/zlib does not change existing disk/network formats.

Following subsequent user instructions, validate adapter tests/installed consumers only in Release. Disable upstream tests/examples/benchmarks/docs and check only required declarations, linkage, and API boundaries. Adding Folly/Fizz/Boost/OpenSSL/fmt/fast_float/gflags/glog/libevent/libsodium under `deps/` for mvfst, reusing existing Zstandard/zlib. Connect explicit required layers instead of fetching the whole upstream getdeps test-only/optional closure. Build only context/regex Boost binaries.

The environment is Linux x86-64/GCC 13.3.0/CMake 3.28.3. Actual executions and limitations follow.

### core/uv Modules and Active-header Generation (2026-11-03)

Removed root `src/` and reorganized the library into `modules/core` and `modules/uv`. Each owns `include/`, `privates/`, `src/`, and `CMakeLists.txt`. Core has 28 public headers under `modules/core/include/raftpp/`; UV has seven under `modules/uv/include/raftpp/uv/`. Sources number 28 core/13 UV, including conditional sources; private headers number seven core/nine UV. Traced the public-value-type dependency closure, retaining only seven required core support headers under public `detail/` and excluding other internals from installation.

Core provides consensus/values-codecs/errors-memory-tracing/abstract I-O-transport/optional fixtures-backtraces. UV provides built-in file I-O/TCP/streams/LZ4 frame detection-decoding. Preserved the `raftpp` type-function namespace, callbacks/ownership/disk-network formats. Flat paths such as `<raftpp/uv_io.hpp>` become `<raftpp/uv/uv_io.hpp>`. Compression helpers belong to UV. UV OFF omits those headers/functions and the compression test registration; UV ON/LZ4 OFF preserves decoding's `invalid` behavior.

Build targets are `raftpp::core`, optional `raftpp::uv`, and aggregate interface `raftpp::raftpp`. Each module configures its sources/options/links/exports in its own CMake file. Shared symbols use core's `RAFTPP_API` and UV's `RAFTPP_UV_API`; the core assertion symbol required by private UV calls is also exported. The aggregate `raftpp` build goal builds enabled modules. Installed module files use `libraftpp-core`/`libraftpp-uv` with platform suffixes.

Root `include/` is a generated mirror added to `.gitignore`. Configure stages module originals/generated configuration-export headers/the aggregate entry point from `cmake/raftpp.hpp.in`. Compilers/installation use only each build's `<build>/include/`, avoiding another configuration's shared mirror. Lock mirror updates, remove disabled headers, and preserve unchanged header timestamps to avoid unrelated recompilation. Updated VS Code/examples/tools/tests/installation paths and paired API documents. Restored the missing upstream license from the existing backup, with original SHA-256 `79d77bb9421aea2a322bd8b7e096a7efddec595fadfbcc484f951bee8063328f`.

Reference scope covers recursive public includes/internal calls-cleanup/feature branches/test-tool private dependencies/installed consumers. Actual product edits relocate files/includes/exports; all 41 implementation files have identical tokens excluding includes/comments/whitespace (`/tmp/raftpp-modules-token-check.json`). Did not rewrite algorithms or asynchronous lifetime contracts.

### C-source Removal, Independent C++ Checks, and Formatting (2026-11-03)

Following the user's request and explicit choice to remove C comparisons and convert to independent C++ tests, removed the entire `raft/` tree and C reference target/generated headers/comparison bridges. No external C oracle is required. Before deletion the source was commit `148951f79a1ed529d6f112661a3067494f1a0917`; original-source links in historical mappings now point to that upstream commit. C-comparison/206-registration/old-hash records below describe the period before removal, not fresh implementation or runtime evidence.

Actual changes:

- `CMakeLists.txt` enables only `LANGUAGES CXX`. Provides only `raftpp::raftpp`, removing C sources/targets/`raft.h` generation/search paths. Linux-header and GNU libbacktrace probes also use C++. Existing libuv/LZ4/fixture/backtrace/static-shared/examples/benchmark/install selection rules remain.
- Removed sixteen C sources/headers from `tests/`. Only required ENOSPC/allocation syscall injection remains as `fallocate.cpp`, `file_allocation_hooks.cpp`, and `file_allocation.hpp`, as independent test helpers. Renamed comparison-only registrations to `contracts` and removed duplicate `legacy_transfer_cpp_{normal,forced}` checks. The full configuration registers 204 checks, the core/fixture profile 99. Registrations are not fresh pass counts.
- Configuration/trail/log checks use independent `vector`/`map` models. CRC/SHA-1/random/configuration/RPC-format checks use fixed vectors and round-trip/incremental/boundary conditions. Core/node/fixture checks cover state/durable indices/membership/callbacks/failure output/ownership. Retained all 171 fixed-seed fuzzy combinations, setting a valid election timeout per tick instead of inspecting C's timer internals.
- File/recovery checks use submitted entries/snapshots/metadata, specified errors/boundaries and repeat-load stability; writer/allocation fallback checks cover file bytes/write positions-counts/cleanup. TCP/stream interoperability uses POSIX peers. TCP checks all six RPCs in both directions plus handshake/frame bytes; resolver retains nine multiple-address cases. These independent checks are not presented as the same method as earlier per-event C comparisons.
- Added `.clang-format` and formatted 159 C++ files with clang-format 18.1.8. Uses LLVM-based four-space/100-column rules, disabling include/using reordering, comment reflow and string splitting. The 112 product headers/sources/examples/tools have identical tokens to the pre-removal versions after excluding comments/whitespace. Algorithms/public APIs/disk-network formats are unchanged.
- Preserved the original license byte-for-byte in [licenses/cowsql-raft.LICENSE](LICENSE.md) (SHA-256 `79d77bb9421aea2a322bd8b7e096a7efddec595fadfbcc484f951bee8063328f`). Updated attribution comments/install paths/paired instructions/CMake APIs. Configured VS Code's C++ formatter/format-on-save and regenerated default `build/compile_commands.json` (115 entries, zero C sources). Removed old `libraft`/generated C headers and C object artifacts.

Reference tracing covered C targets/generated headers through every test bridge/caller, native error injection, feature branches and installed consumers. Actual product edits are formatting/attribution comments; build/test/instruction/progress/relevant API documents were edited separately. Did not translate the original tree or put progress records into API documents.

Core used `cmake --build /tmp/raftpp-cxx-tests-core --parallel 2`, then `ctest --test-dir /tmp/raftpp-cxx-tests-core --output-on-failure --output-log /tmp/raftpp-c-removal-core-tests-initial.log -E '^raftpp\.(assertions|foundation_failure|core_failure|core_stale_vote|core_index_limits|core_term_limits|core_clock_limits)$'`. Excluded seven unchanged checks and reran only failing log/progress as corrected. Native used `cmake --build /tmp/raftpp-cxx-tests-native --parallel 2`, then selected twenty-nine file-allocation/probe/snapshot-capacity/segment-buffer-log-append/writer/metadata-snapshot-segment-recovery-limits/native-errors/package checks, plus `ctest --test-dir /tmp/raftpp-cxx-tests-native -R '^raftpp\.(stream_interop|tcp_interop|tcp_resolver_contracts)$' --output-on-failure`. Each native/core package_install built and ran an independent consumer from a relocated installation.

Preserved initial failures: corrected a leftover lambda replacement in the initial core compile, and removed two helpers used only by C comparisons after native strict-build warnings (`...core-build-initial.log`, `...native-build-initial.log`, `...native-build-cleanup.log`). Two core runtime failures were new-model snapshot/last-boundary and progress never-sent/timestamp precondition errors. The new TCP peer's accept predicate overwrote an already acquired FD when called again; after correction, reran only tcp_interop successfully. These are not product bug fixes. Did not repeat other passing checks.

Final cleanup renames test-internal `reference`/`compare_c` helpers to `contracts`/`check_descriptors` and clarifies diagnostics. Conditions/inputs/CLI registrations remain unchanged, so only the affected targets are recompiled and passing runtime results reused.

There is no current C-library/comparison dependency. Preserve past C comparisons/full-port auditing as evidence from their original time. Actual perf/trace/raw-device/all-filesystem-permission/32-bit/LSan limitations and the historic first TCP timeout/additional convergence condition are not reported as resolved. This cleanup did not run a new whole suite/full option matrix/ASan-UBSan.

The earlier storage-value unit completed implementation and default, option-specific, and ASan/UBSan verification of logs, entries, shared batches, snapshot value copying, and aligned buffers. Static and shared external-consumer linking also passed. The inspected scope includes `log.c`, `entry.c`, `snapshot.c`, and ownership and release paths in `legacy.c`, `uv_encoding.c`, `uv_send.c`, and `uv_append.c`. Those historical storage-value checks do not establish coverage of the subsequent consensus-core or asynchronous-I/O units. API contracts are documented in [Korean](docs/ko/storage.md) and [English](docs/en/storage.md).

C-source links in this table are entry points for each area. Trace related headers, calls, deallocation, callbacks, and conditional compilation; inspecting a linked file alone does not establish coverage of the entire area. Track the implementation and verification scopes described above separately.

Election and replication-progress helpers, RequestVote handling, and follower conversion are implemented and verified within the scope recorded below. Reference scope includes `progress.c`, `election.c`, `random.c`, `message.c`, and their call, state, and error paths in `convert.c`, `timeout.c`, `recv_request_vote*.c`, and `replication.c`. That election unit covers internal state and RPC value types, election timers, election start, voting, tallying, replication-progress algorithms, and term updates and follower conversion on RequestVote reception. Node event dispatch, candidate and leader conversion, and actual transport are outside this unit.

Candidate and leader conversion, persistence, replication, membership, snapshots, and event-driven core integration are implemented and passed default, option-specific, and ASan/UBSan verification. Static and shared external-consumer linking also passed. Reference scope includes `convert.c`, `replication.c`, `client.c`, `membership.c`, `restore.c`, `timeout.c`, `recv_*.c`, `raft.c`, and message, persistence-completion, and log-ownership paths in `legacy.c`. The external libuv backend and public callback node API are separate from this core unit.

Public node, io, and state_machine callback APIs and their event driver are implemented and verified with twenty-eight default tests, six feature combinations, ASan/UBSan, and external static and shared consumers. Leak checks were excluded. API contracts are documented in [Korean](docs/ko/node.md) and [English](docs/en/node.md). Reference scope covers startup, persistence completion, FSM application, promotion, snapshots, and close paths in legacy.c, raft.c, and public I/O, FSM, and request declarations. Libuv and file backends remain separate implementation work.

Message and log-batch encoding, CRC32, SHA-1, and digest are implemented and verified with thirty-three default tests, six feature combinations, ASan/UBSan, and external static and shared consumers. Leak checks were excluded. Reference scope covers byte, padding, checksum, and ownership paths in `byte.c`, `uv_encoding.c`, `uv_recv.c`, `uv_send.c`, `uv_append.c`, `uv_segment.c`, and `raft_digest`. Actual socket and file backends and full segment-recovery policy remain separate work.

Persistent metadata and snapshot files are implemented and verified with thirty-nine default tests, six feature combinations, ASan/UBSan, and external static and shared consumers. Leak checks were excluded. Reference scope traces dual-slot recovery, fsync/fdatasync, anonymous temporary files, publication, cleanup, and compressed reads in `uv_metadata.c`, `uv_snapshot.c`, `uv_fs.c`, `uv_os.c`, and `uv.c`. Segment-file recovery, asynchronous barriers, and actual socket transport remain separate work.

## Feature Options and Coverage

| Option | Current coverage | Verification conditions and limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `RAFTPP_WITH_UV` | Connects files/KAIO writer/probes/log-snapshot coordinators/public uv_io/TCP-raw-stream adapters and owning per-request diagnostics. | Requires Linux/libuv and the necessary headers. OFF excludes these sources/header contents/external dependencies/checks. |
| `RAFTPP_WITH_LZ4` | Connects frame detection/decompression and existing compressed-snapshot reads. Also verified the decoded-payload path through native TCP catch-up, receiver persistence and restart. | Requires UV. Reading a compressed file with OFF returns invalid. The snapshot-compression setter remains the original no-op; no compressor is added. |
| `RAFTPP_WITH_FIXTURE` | Connects an independent virtual-time fixture and network/disk/membership/snapshot injection-failure/actual-C comparisons. | OFF excludes fixture sources/public-header contents/related checks. Examples/benchmarks do not require fixture. The reference C TIMEOUT_NOW statistics-array defect remains a separate limit. |
| `RAFTPP_WITH_BACKTRACE` | Defaults OFF and is independent of other features. Connects GNU libbacktrace/__assert_fail checks, generated flags and independent C++ assertion output. | Applies to assertion failures in library builds without NDEBUG. Symbol/source resolution depends on the binary/debug information. |
| `RAFTPP_BUILD_TESTS` | Selects feature-specific CTest registrations and C bridges/actual-file-TCP/lifetime-failure/input-boundary checks. | Current registration counts and execution/reuse follow the relevant unit records below. Registrations are not a full-suite pass count. |
| `RAFTPP_BUILD_EXAMPLES` | Defaults OFF and requires UV. Connects public C++ counter/server/process-cluster shutdown and restart. | Builds without tests/fixture/LZ4. Reuses earlier minimal-configuration/option-rejection checks. |
| `RAFTPP_BUILD_BENCHMARKS` | Defaults OFF and requires UV. Connects submit/disk/standard statistics-JSON/io_uring/device discovery/kernel perf-trace tools. | Independent of examples/tests/fixture/LZ4. Actual raw-device/NVMe-nullb kernel perf-trace remains unverified due to environmental limits. |
| `RAFTPP_INSTALL` | Defaults ON at top level/OFF with add_subdirectory. Installs the C++ target/headers/upstream license and relocatable find_package configuration. | Reuses four independent relocated-consumer configurations, missing-dependency/space-path/ON-to-OFF records. The C reference library is not exported. |
| `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS` | Supports static/shared C++ and reference-C targets and C++ public-symbol/external-consumer linking. | Recheck affected consumers when new public APIs or link requirements change. Existing result scope follows each unit record. |

`RAFTPP_WITH_LZ4=ON` requires `RAFTPP_WITH_UV=ON`. Reconfiguring with options disabled also removes previously generated optional C headers. Generated headers express build configuration, not port completion.

## Verification Record

Foundation, log and storage-value, election, event-core, and public-node driver checks are historical records from earlier work. Byte-format, checksum, metadata/snapshot/segment-file, and low-level asynchronous-writer checks are historical as well; current log-writing-coordinator verification is recorded separately below.

- The default Debug configuration passed `raftpp.buffer`, `raftpp.configuration`, `raftpp.trail`, and `raftpp.compression`.
- Configuration tests compared results and errors across 600 mutations with the C implementation, checked encoded bytes and padding, and decoded in both directions. They also checked state preservation for truncated input, oversized counts, and insufficient output space.
- Term-trail tests compared 10,000 append, truncation, snapshot, and restore operations with C. These results do not constitute verification of the entire consensus algorithm.
- LZ4-enabled tests covered known-size and unknown-size frames, output limits, truncated frames, checksum errors, and empty frames. Disabled configurations checked error reporting and output preservation.
- Builds, tests, optional headers, and linked symbols were checked for the six combinations below. Both static and shared modes were included, but this was not a full twelve-combination check of both modes for every feature combination.

| UV | LZ4 | Fixture | Verified link mode |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OFF | OFF | OFF | Shared |
| OFF | OFF | ON | Static |
| ON | OFF | OFF | Static |
| ON | OFF | ON | Shared |
| ON | ON | OFF | Shared |
| ON | ON | ON | Static |

- Reconfiguration from enabled to disabled features and configure rejection of `UV=OFF, LZ4=ON` were checked. Core-only configurations were tested with PkgConfig discovery disabled.
- An external CMake project linked only `raftpp::raftpp` and built and ran configuration encoding, decoding, and decompression API calls. These C++ implementations do not delegate linking to the original C library.
- Foundation tests passed under ASan/UBSan. LeakSanitizer failed because of the environment's ptrace-related restrictions, so leak checking was disabled. There is no passing leak-check result.

### Log and Storage-Value Port Verification (2026-11-02)

- All nine tests passed in the default Debug configuration. The added tests are `raftpp.shared_buffer`, `raftpp.entries`, `raftpp.log`, `raftpp.log_lifetime`, and `raftpp.snapshot`.
- The in-memory log was compared against C across 6,000 append, truncation, snapshot, restore, acquisition, and release operations. Retained entries' metadata and bytes were compared throughout. Separate checks covered busy and retry-after-release behavior for the same `(term,index)` and replacement with a different term.
- Deep entry copying, unpadded batch layout, and single-buffer snapshot compaction were compared against C. Checks also covered self-copying, empty payloads and input, final-reference batch release, alignment, moves, and slice boundaries.
- C++ RAII expresses the original manual-release model's external contracts. Acquired handles retain entries and batches after the in-memory log is destroyed. The new log's `discard` releases shared references while preserving payloads shared by the caller. Lifetime integration with actual asynchronous requests is outside this verification scope.
- All nine tests, optional headers, and linked symbols were rechecked for each of the six feature and link-mode combinations above. Release C++ code used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Checks also covered reconfiguration to disabled features, core-only builds without PkgConfig, and rejection of invalid UV/LZ4 combinations.
- All nine tests passed under ASan/UBSan. The previously observed environment restriction required `detect_leaks=0`; leak checking was not performed.
- An external CMake project linked only `raftpp::raftpp` and built and ran the new buffer, entry, and snapshot APIs. Checks covered a shared core-only configuration and an LZ4-enabled static configuration; link commands did not include the original C library.
- Korean and English API code examples and relative links were checked. The reference implementation under `raft/` was unchanged.

### Election and Replication-Progress Port Verification (2026-11-02)

- All fifteen tests passed in the default Debug configuration. The added tests are `raftpp.random`, `raftpp.progress`, `raftpp.election`, `raftpp.vote`, `raftpp.vote_request`, and `raftpp.messages`.
- Random outputs and subsequent state were compared against C for four seeds across valid ranges and the full 32-bit range. The C reference divides by zero for equal bounds; C++ returns the sole value without advancing state. Normal election sequences are unchanged.
- Replication progress was compared against C across 12,000 steps covering probe, pipeline, snapshot transitions, acknowledgments, rejections, timestamps, capabilities, catch-up, and snapshot deadlines. Checks also covered per-ID state preservation on configuration reordering, addition, and removal, and index-overflow rejection.
- C comparisons covered 200 election starts for each pre-vote mode, tallying including duplicate votes, 1,000 follower-timer resets, and 15,000 vote decisions across voter, standby, and spare roles. Requests were checked to advertise only the persisted prefix.
- A total of 3,000 RequestVote requests were compared with the public C `raft_step()` path. Checks covered roles, terms, votes, replies, timers, and update flags across follower, candidate, and leader states, higher and lower terms, existing leaders, pre-vote, disrupt_leader, and snapshot installation.
- Messages were checked to retain addresses, configurations, and payloads independently of configuration-object lifetime. Standard variants clean up role-specific vectors and strings. Integration with actual transport completion callbacks remains outstanding.
- All fifteen tests, optional headers, and linked symbols were rechecked for each of the six feature and link-mode combinations above. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Checks covered core-only builds without PkgConfig, reconfiguration to disabled features, and invalid UV/LZ4 rejection.
- All fifteen tests passed under ASan/UBSan. Leak checks were excluded due to the previously observed environment restriction. Only the C-comparison executable links the reference C library.
- Public APIs did not change, so API documentation was unchanged. Korean and English progress counterparts and relative links were checked. The reference tree, `raft/`, was unchanged. Allocation-failure injection, tracing integration, and full-node integration verification remain outstanding.

### Event-Driven Core Port Verification (2026-11-02)

- All twenty-two tests passed in the default Debug configuration. The added tests are `raftpp.core_election`, `raftpp.core_replication`, `raftpp.core_conflict`, `raftpp.core_membership`, `raftpp.core_snapshot`, `raftpp.core_cluster`, and `raftpp.core_failure`.
- The internal `step()` connects startup, receive, persistence completion, configuration application, snapshots, timeouts, submission, catch-up, and leadership transfer. Each event is also passed to the public C `raft_step()` to compare roles, terms, votes, log terms, commits, configurations, progress, timers, update flags, RPC metadata and counts, and entry and snapshot bytes to persist.
- Checks covered initial barriers, automatic single-voter election, local and remote persistence and quorum, duplicate requests and responses, conflict truncation, shutdown on committed conflicts, capacity limits, configuration rollback and commit, spare catch-up, and TimeoutNow transfer.
- An in-memory three-node test ran 400 commands for each pre-vote mode with loss, duplication, and reordering. Leadership transfer, an uncommitted minority-leader write, majority reelection, matching commits and payloads after partition recovery, and preservation of the previously committed prefix were compared with C. This test uses simulated persistence-completion events; actual disk and network verification remains outstanding.
- Standard C++ allocation failures were injected at allocation limits 0 through 79 on each of four paths: command submission, configuration submission, configuration receipt, and snapshot acceptance. Checks covered rolling back incomplete log, configuration, commit, and persistence updates and successful retries. Snapshot configuration values are prepared before submission, so durable-state installation completes even if allocating its acknowledgment message fails.
- Transport and persistence updates own shared entry handles. Checks covered simultaneous retired and replacement entries, retained request data surviving core destruction, and storage release after the final handle. Actual backend request and completion-callback integration is a separate task.
- All twenty-two tests, optional headers, and linked symbols passed for each of the six option and link-mode combinations above. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Checks covered core builds without PkgConfig, reconfiguration to disabled features, and invalid UV/LZ4 rejection. Intermediate objects built concurrently with state-type edits were removed, and the full matrix was rechecked against stable final sources.
- All twenty-two tests passed under ASan/UBSan. The environment restriction required `detect_leaks=0`; this is not a passing leak-check result.
- An external CMake project linked only raftpp::raftpp and ran core startup, submission, and persistence completion. Checks covered an LZ4-enabled static configuration and a shared configuration without optional dependencies, including retained data after core destruction and final release. Link commands did not include the reference C library.
- Public umbrella APIs were unchanged. Work targeted internal headers and the event core; API documentation was retained and Korean and English progress counterparts were updated together. Original C sources were unchanged.

### Public Callback Node Driver Verification (2026-11-02)

- All twenty-eight tests passed in the default Debug configuration. The new public node tests are `raftpp.node_lifetime`, `raftpp.node_failure`, `raftpp.node_snapshot`, `raftpp.node_cluster`, `raftpp.node_restore`, and `raftpp.node_allocation`.
- Connected startup, persistence completion, FSM application, membership promotion, snapshots, and close paths from `legacy.c` in `node.cpp`. Added public `io` and `state_machine` interfaces and a move-only `node` handle.
- Checks covered commands, barriers, addition, promotion, removal, leadership transfer, responses after term and vote persistence, inline and deferred completion, no callback on immediate failure, and exactly one completion for accepted requests. Failed term persistence prevents vote recording and responses. The pre-start state preserves the reference `raft_init` behavior, `follower`. Overload checks also cover `nullptr`, null function pointers, and ordinary function references.
- Checked callback reentrancy and node destruction, move-only callbacks and buffers, pending-I/O and FSM lifetimes, and final release after asynchronous close. Injected standard-allocation failures at positions 0 through 79 and checked immediate failure, completion, and close-registration failure. One single-command completion-bookkeeping path was checked without additional allocation; this does not establish allocation-free completion for every path.
- A three-node public API test with mock storage and transport covered elections, commands, spare addition, snapshot loading, sending, installation, catch-up promotion, removal, and transfer. Checks also covered startup restoration of multi-buffer snapshots, preservation and later commit of an uncommitted suffix, installation waiting for a local snapshot, and exactly one finalization after second-phase FSM failure, storage submission and completion failures, and cancellation. Actual disk and socket backends and C++ fixture verification remain outstanding.
- After the final callback-overload fix, all twenty-eight tests passed under ASan/UBSan. The environment restriction required `detect_leaks=0`; leak checking was not performed.
- An external CMake project linked only `raftpp::raftpp` and exercised public node startup, moved buffers, move-only completion, FSM results, and close. Checks covered a shared configuration without optional dependencies and an LZ4-enabled static configuration.
- Final sources passed all twenty-eight tests, optional-header checks, and linked-symbol checks for each of the six feature and link-mode combinations above. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Checks also covered core configurations without optional dependencies, reconfiguration from enabled to disabled features, and rejection of invalid UV/LZ4 combinations.
- API contracts were added in both `docs/ko/node.md` and `docs/en/node.md`. Matching paths, reciprocal links, and C++20 example compilation were checked. Original sources under `raft/` were unchanged.

### Byte-Format and Checksum Port Verification (2026-11-02)

- All thirty-three tests passed in the default Debug configuration. The new tests are `raftpp.checksums`, `raftpp.wire`, `raftpp.records`, `raftpp.encoding_malformed`, and `raftpp.encoding_failure`.
- Compared CRC32 and incrementally chunked SHA-1 against C across 600 inputs. Also compared `raft_digest` with matching text and 64-bit n. Checked the SHA-1 `abc` and million-`a` standard vectors, repeated digest, continued updates, and reset. Incremental state uses standard arrays and integers without dynamic allocation.
- For 600 inputs covering all six RPCs, compared the C encoder's preamble, header, payload bytes, and buffer count, plus C header-decoder entry metadata and payload lengths. Checked full C++ encode/decode round trips, shared received-entry storage, and final-reference release. Public node mock transport now passes through this codec for elections, commands, snapshots, promotion, and transfer.
- Compared 400 disk records with actual output from the unchanged C segment-buffer encoder. This verifies in-memory record formats, not file I/O or recovery policy. UV-disabled configurations run C++ round-trip and error checks without libuv; UV-enabled configurations add C wire and segment comparison sources. The comparison target uses function sections and linker GC to retain only the relevant record-buffer routines.
- Checked every truncated RPC and record length, every record-byte corruption, oversized counts, configured limits, invalid types, older versions, and 5,000 random malformed inputs. Injected standard-allocation failures at positions 0 through 79 on each of eight encoding/decoding paths, checking output and consumed preservation on failure and successful calls when allocation succeeds.
- Preserves the C store's eight-byte entry-length requirement, CRCs, and little-endian formats. Network payloads are concatenated without padding, matching C transmission bytes. C++ does not apply the old C receive helper's padded pointer offsets to unaligned payloads or reproduce its out-of-bounds flags read for legacy 32-byte RequestVote. C++ SHA-1 digest queries do not consume state; string_view digest hashes its entire specified length, including embedded NUL. The free digest function matches C for ordinary C strings.
- All thirty-three tests passed under ASan/UBSan. The previously observed environment restriction required `detect_leaks=0`; leak checking was excluded.
- An external CMake consumer linked only the C++ target and ran checksum and digest, RPC encode/decode, record encoding, and shared-payload decoding. Both the shared configuration without optional dependencies and the LZ4-enabled static configuration passed.
- Final sources passed all thirty-three tests, optional-header checks, and linked-symbol checks for each of the six feature and link-mode combinations above. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Core builds without PkgConfig, disabled-feature reconfiguration, and invalid UV/LZ4 rejection also passed. That byte-format unit did not cover actual segment files, snapshot metadata, compressed-file reads, socket handshakes, or backend integration.
- Added API contracts and examples to `docs/ko/encoding.md` and `docs/en/encoding.md`, and checked C++20 compilation of both language examples and document links. Original sources under `raft/` are preserved.

### Metadata and Snapshot-File Port Verification (2026-11-02)

- All thirty-nine default Debug tests passed. `raftpp.metadata_formats` runs regardless of feature options. UV adds `raftpp.metadata_files`, `raftpp.snapshot_files`, `raftpp.disk_failures`, `raftpp.compressed_snapshot`, and `raftpp.disk_no_space`. Configurations without UV have thirty-four tests in total.
- Independently implemented synchronous file operations using initialization and term/vote paths in `uv_metadata.c`, `uv_snapshot.c`, `uv_fs.c`, `uv_os.c`, and `uv.c`. Verified generation selection between metadata1/metadata2, short-file removal and prior-slot recovery, oversized-file and equal-generation rejection, new-file fsync→close→directory-fsync ordering, and existing-file fdatasync→close ordering. Only successful records update the cache; uncertainty after file mutation stops further metadata writes in that instance.
- Implemented snapshot metadata CRC/configuration format and term/index/timestamp filenames, anonymous `O_TMPFILE` preparation and content fsync, metadata→data publication followed by directory fsync, orphan/tmp cleanup, and retaining the newest two snapshots. Barrier coordination between preparation and publication is not yet included. Checked moving and discarding prepared objects, lifetime beyond the store object, and actual descriptor counts.
- C successfully read C++ metadata and snapshots; C++ read C metadata and snapshot fixtures. The C snapshot fixture uses its configuration encoder, byte helpers, and synchronized file writer, not the asynchronous C snapshot driver. Both implementations rejected corrupted snapshot metadata. Filename collisions preserve existing pairs.
- Checked 500 metadata/snapshot-format round trips, truncated lengths, CRC corruption, zero generations, invalid formats, and output preservation on allocation failure. Injected open, write, allocate, fsync, fdatasync, close, publication, and directory-sync failures into actual file operations. Snapshot preparation also released handles under standard-allocation failures at positions 0 through 79.
- Added native posix_fallocate ENOSPC injection to verify the same no_space status as C, correcting an initial C++ mapping to io_error. The test-only interposer forwards normal calls to libc and returns ENOSPC only for this check. Tests also cover enabled LZ4 snapshot reads and disabled-feature errors and output preservation.
- All thirty-nine final tests passed under ASan/UBSan. The existing environment restriction required `detect_leaks=0`; leak checking was excluded. External consumers exercised metadata codecs in a shared configuration without optional dependencies, and actual file writes, snapshot publication, and reopening in an LZ4-enabled static configuration. All six final feature and link-mode combinations passed: thirty-nine tests each with UV, thirty-four each without UV. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`; checks also confirmed excluded C++ libuv symbols/dependencies when disabled and rejection of invalid UV/LZ4 combinations. Actual power-cut behavior and durability on every filesystem were not tested; verification covers formats, explicit synchronization order, partial files, and failure cleanup. Segment files and recovery, asynchronous I/O coordination, and network transport remain outstanding.
- Added the new public metadata codecs to both Korean and English API documentation and checked links and example compilation. disk_storage and prepared_snapshot are internal file-layer components, not a complete public I/O backend. Original C sources are preserved.

### Segment-File and Stored-State Recovery Verification (2026-11-02)

- All forty-three default Debug tests passed. UV-enabled configurations add `raftpp.segment_files`, `raftpp.segment_recovery`, `raftpp.segment_snapshot`, and `raftpp.segment_failures`. UV-disabled configurations retain the existing thirty-four tests.
- Traced `uv_segment.c`, `uv_list.c`, `uv_truncate.c`, `uv_finalize.c`, `uv.c`, and related file, batch, configuration, and release paths. Independently implemented temporary closed-segment creation→file fsync→rename→directory fsync, reading with shared batch lifetime, valid-prefix recovery of open files, empty-file removal, closed-name finalization, trailing retention, and suffix truncation in detail/disk.hpp and disk.cpp.
- Full stored-state loading includes newest-to-oldest snapshot fallback, selection of the newest contiguous segment chunk, snapshot-gap checks, numeric open-counter sorting, quarantine of the damaged final segment and subsequent open files, and one retry on corruption. Bootstrap persists the term before writing the configuration entry; recover appends a new configuration using the last log term. Asynchronous submission/completion, barriers, and direct I/O are outside this unit.
- segments.cpp compares entries and payloads in eighty consecutive segments with C. It exercises actual synchronous C load/bootstrap/recover entry points and compares outcomes and recovered file bytes for open files, CRC corruption, zero padding, short input, malformed types, invalid counts, middle/final corruption, gaps, snapshot fallback, and boundary selection. The reference bridge does not initialize transport or timer handles, so this is not verification of an actual asynchronous backend. Truncation compares C prefix rewriting and the worker's subsequent file-removal order separately.
- Injected scan, open, read, write, fsync, close, publication, truncate, rename, remove, and directory-sync failures. Checked output preservation, descriptor cleanup, and retry with allocation failures at positions 0–99 for loading and 0–69 for file creation. Also checked C's limitation that failed deletion can leave both the original segment and its already-persisted truncated replacement. Open-file recovery also synchronizes the directory after removal or rename; failures of this additional synchronization are returned as I/O errors. This does not establish atomic multi-file transactions or power-cut recovery.
- Allocation-failure injection and a backtrace identified termination inside libstdc++ directory_iterator. Only directory scanning uses an RAII request from the existing libuv dependency to preserve the error-return contract; paths, containers, strings, sorting, and batch ownership use standard implementations. Closed-file indices are checked against C's sixteen-digit format; overflows, duplicate counters, invalid ranges, and overlaps when loading without a snapshot are safely rejected.
- Public user APIs did not change; this unit belongs to the internal detail file layer. API-only docs/ was unchanged. All forty-three tests also passed under ASan/UBSan; leak checking was excluded due to environment restrictions. External consumers also passed in shared core-only, shared UV/LZ4, and static UV/LZ4 configurations, exercising the new file layer with the C++ library. All six feature combinations passed forty-three tests with UV and thirty-four without, plus optional-header and symbol checks. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Disabled-feature reconfiguration, core builds without PkgConfig, and rejection of LZ4 without UV also passed. Original C sources are preserved.

### Asynchronous File-Writer Verification (2026-11-02)

- All forty-eight default Debug tests passed. UV-enabled configurations add `raftpp.writer_basic`, `raftpp.writer_async`, `raftpp.writer_close`, `raftpp.writer_failure`, and `raftpp.writer_allocation`. UV-disabled configurations retain thirty-four tests. All forty-eight ASan/UBSan tests and external shared core-only, shared UV/LZ4, and static UV/LZ4 consumers passed. Leak checking was excluded due to environment restrictions. All six feature combinations passed forty-eight tests with UV and thirty-four without, plus optional-header and symbol checks. Release C++ used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`; disabled-feature reconfiguration, core builds without PkgConfig, and rejection of LZ4 without UV also passed. After the final cumulative-length/offset checks, all forty-eight default tests and the five strict Release and ASan/UBSan writer tests passed again.
- Traced uv_writer.c, uv_writer.h, uv_os.c, syscall.c, and submission/close callers in uv_append.c. file_writer implements RWF_DSYNC scatter writes, direct I/O, RWF_NOWAIT with eventfd/UV polling, submission/completion EAGAIN threadpool retries, and concurrent requests. Extracted the existing native-file RAII into file_handle for reuse by synchronous storage. No coroutine APIs were added.
- Standard ownership types and list retain requests, iovecs, and shared buffers. Removing a request before invoking its callback permits reentrant submission, with payloads alive throughout the callback. Accepted requests survive close and owner destruction; workers perform kernel io_destroy and descriptor closure. Polled cancellations run after kernel use of buffers ends, and close completion follows release of requests, library-owned buffer references, and native handles. Worker requests deliver their normal completion.
- Compared buffered/direct file bytes with the original C writer. The C capability probe reported block size 4096 and NOWAIT support; actual polled completions, multiple requests, and submission/completion EAGAIN fallbacks ran. C++ traverses the entire event batch instead of the original poll callback's early return after EAGAIN, which can skip remaining completions. It does not reproduce C's cancellation-memory release before kernel teardown. Consensus algorithms and disk formats are unchanged.
- Checked initialization, queueing, submission, eventfd reads, completion results, cleanup-queue failure, negative I/O versus short-write status, busy/offset and cumulative-length validation, callback exceptions, and cleanup retry. Cumulative write length uses uint64_t independently of size_t width. Allocation sweeps cover positions 0–19 for initialization and 0–24 for submission, plus allocation-free close, moves, owner destruction, pending worker/poll close, and exactly-once completion. Descriptor comparisons run after libuv creates its process-global signal pipe.
- With RAFTPP_WITH_UV=ON, raftpp::raftpp propagates libuv header/link usage requirements, documented in both node API translations. The writer itself is an internal detail component. C++ filesystem capability probing, prepare pools, append/barrier coordination, upper I/O backend, and tracing integration remain outstanding. Original C sources are preserved.

Default verification commands:

```sh
cmake -S . -B build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DRAFTPP_BUILD_TESTS=ON
cmake --build build --parallel
ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure
```

### Log-Writing, Barrier, and Truncation Coordinator Verification (2026-11-02)

- All fifty-six default Debug and ASan/UBSan tests passed. UV-enabled configurations add `raftpp.segment_buffer`, `raftpp.disk_probe`, `raftpp.log_append`, `raftpp.log_barrier`, `raftpp.log_close`, `raftpp.log_failure`, `raftpp.log_allocation`, and `raftpp.native_node`. A Release build with `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror` and the eight new tests also passed. LeakSanitizer was excluded with `detect_leaks=0` due to previously established environment restrictions.
- All six feature/link-mode combinations in the table above passed fifty-six tests with UV or thirty-four without, optional-header checks, and linked-symbol checks. Release C++ builds used `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. Checks also covered disabling previously enabled features, a core-only configuration without PkgConfig discovery, and rejection of LZ4 without UV. This does not constitute twelve-combination verification of both static and shared modes for every option combination.
- Reference scope covers `uv_fs.c`, `uv_os.c`, `uv_segment.c`, `uv_prepare.c`, `uv_append.c`, `uv_finalize.c`, `uv_truncate.c`, and call, request, cancellation, and close paths in `uv.c`, `uv_snapshot.c`, and `legacy.c`. Actual changes cover internal probing, batch staging, prepare pools, append/finalization, barriers, truncation, file allocation, and failed writer-initialization cleanup. Inspecting snapshot callers does not mean upper snapshot coordination is implemented.
- Added [probe.hpp](modules/uv/privates/probe.hpp)/[probe.cpp](modules/uv/src/probe.cpp), [segment_buffer.hpp](modules/uv/privates/segment_buffer.hpp)/[segment_buffer.cpp](modules/uv/src/segment_buffer.cpp), and [log_writer.hpp](modules/uv/privates/log_writer.hpp)/[log_writer.cpp](modules/uv/src/log_writer.cpp). Probing determines direct-I/O alignment and NOWAIT support. Batch staging retains the final partial block, and an aligned submission copy remains immutable through completion/retries. This makes no claim of eliminating copies or improving performance.
- Implemented two idle prepared files, one preparation worker at a time, ordered append/finalization, per-operation retry deadlines, and barriers waiting for preceding segments. Blocking barriers pause subsequent writes; nonblocking barriers permit them. Suffix truncation uses a blocking barrier and worker. Truncation at or after the next append index succeeds immediately, supporting the existing node driver's truncate call before every append.
- [log_writer.cpp](tests/log_writer.cpp) and `tests/log_writer_reference.c` (removed) compare closed-segment names/full bytes from the actual native C backend, C batch buffers, and capability results. Checks cover buffered 512-byte and automatically probed direct/NOWAIT paths, repeated flushes, partial blocks, oversized batches, segment rotation, and reload. The C comparison transport uses no sockets, so this is not network verification.
- Checks cover request ordering, reentrancy, owner destruction, exactly-once completion, close during preparation/writes/truncation, cancellation of unstarted truncation, and close after active truncation completes. Errors after append acceptance are delivered only through completion callbacks. Checks also cover preparation no-space retries, write-completion retries, finalization rename failure and durable-prefix recovery, standard-allocation failures at initialization positions 0–34 and append positions 0–49, and allocation-free close. The upper owner also waits for native cleanup after low-level writer initialization fails.
- A test-only `io` adapter connects the public `node` to actual disk storage: a single voter persists twelve commands, closes inside a completion callback, restarts with a fresh FSM, and applies thirteen commands including the next submission. This adapter supplies neither transport nor snapshot I/O and is not a complete public native backend.
- External consumers linking only `raftpp::raftpp` passed shared core-only, shared UV/LZ4, and static UV/LZ4 configurations. UV configurations chain actual log append→truncate→close from callbacks with the C++ library. New code is excluded by the UV option. No work records were added to API-only `docs/`. Multi-file atomicity and power-loss recovery under file errors, networking, and fixtures are outside this verification scope.
- Checked five Korean/English document pairs for matching sections, tables, code examples except translated comments, and 258 relative links. Reference C sources were unchanged.

### Snapshot Coordinator and Public libuv I/O Verification (2026-11-02)

- The last static option configuration had one three-node failure while submitting to a fixed leader. Twenty repetitions after adding diagnostics did not reproduce it. Updated the test to find the current leader before each command, use a one-second election timeout, explicitly transfer leadership, and check every node's error state. The updated three-node Release test passed five consecutive repetitions, and all sixty-six default and ASan/UBSan tests passed again. The redundant full option rerun was interrupted at the user’s request. The changed shared/LZ4 three-node check subsequently passed alone; production code was unchanged.

- Ten new tests passed: `raftpp.file_snapshots`, `raftpp.file_snapshot_order`, `raftpp.file_snapshot_failure`, `raftpp.file_snapshot_close`, `raftpp.file_snapshot_allocation`, `raftpp.uv_io_lifetime`, `raftpp.uv_io_node`, `raftpp.uv_io_limits`, `raftpp.uv_io_allocation`, and `raftpp.uv_io_cluster`. A Release build with `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror` and the same ten checks also passed. All sixty-six default and ASan/UBSan tests and shared core-only, shared UV/LZ4, and static UV/LZ4 external consumers passed. Leak checking was excluded with `detect_leaks=0` due to environment restrictions. See the baseline table below for executed, reused, and interrupted option scopes.
- Recursive reference scope covers `uv.c`, `uv_snapshot.c`, `uv_append.c`, `uv_finalize.c`, `uv_truncate.c`, original public C I/O/transport declarations, and persistence/installation/callback/close paths in `legacy.c` and `node.cpp`. Implementation covers `file_backend` snapshot preparation retries→barriers→publication/retention, asynchronous get/close, and public `uv_io` file/timer/transport integration. It does not establish implementation of TCP connect/listen, handshakes, or stream receiving/sending.
- Preparation failures retry at the configured disk interval. Local-snapshot barriers permit subsequent writes; installation barriers change the next index and block writes until publication/cleanup completes. Preserve the latest two snapshots and trailing-segment policy. Publication failure completes with an error and does not guarantee rollback. Compared three native C puts each with trailing 0 and 3: filenames with normalized timestamps, closed-segment/metadata/data bytes, and retention results.
- `file_backend` retains requests and shared snapshots/buffers until native work finishes. Close cancels preparation/retry/barrier waits and drains active publication/reads. Added an upper-coordinator notification after asynchronous cleanup of failed log-writer initialization. Library-held request data is released before close completion; it does not wait for user-held references to be released.
- Public `uv_io` connects probing, metadata, bootstrap/recover/load, log writing, truncation, snapshots, time/random, and timers to `io`; `transport` defines message receive/send/close boundaries. Close, owner destruction, and exceptions inside callbacks preserve lifetime and do not cross native boundaries. Active tick/receive callbacks use standard shared ownership. Preserve C's maximum-exclusive `std::rand` range; capacity reporting saturates at the 16-bit maximum instead of wrapping.
- Checks cover preparation no-space retries, publication and preparation/publication/get queue errors, close during preparation/publication/reads, and cancellation during a long retry delay. Standard-allocation failure sweeps cover internal open/put/get positions 0–44 and public init/load/start/send positions 0–54, no callback on immediate failure, allocation-free close, and descriptor cleanup. A startup snapshot-size-limit failure preserves output and initiates shutdown.
- Public nodes restart from actual disk snapshots into fresh FSMs and apply log suffixes. The three-node test uses actual file storage and test message transport/codecs to check election, sixteen committed commands and leadership transfer within the majority during a partition, installation/catch-up from snapshots read from disk, the seventeenth command after healing, full shutdown, and reloading every store. This is not a socket or power-loss recovery test.
- API contracts are in `docs/ko/uv_io.md` and `docs/en/uv_io.md`, linked from node documentation. Abstract `transport` is available without UV; `uv_io`, internal file coordination, related tests, and libuv dependencies are excluded by the UV option. Reference C sources were unchanged. TCP transport, tracing, custom heap/PMR, fixtures, examples/tools, and remaining compatibility settings retain their outstanding scope.

### TCP Transport Unit: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: added `tcp_transport.hpp` and `tcp_transport.cpp`. Recursive reference scope traces handshake, resolution, queues, callbacks, and cleanup in `uv_tcp*.c`, `uv_ip.c`, `uv_send.c`, and `uv_recv.c`, plus public C transport and existing C++ codecs, `transport`, `uv_io`, and node boundaries. Implemented with existing libuv and standard ownership types/containers independently. Original `raft/` sources are unchanged.
- Implements protocol-1 little-endian handshakes and eight-byte address padding, IPv4/default port 8080/bind overrides, listener deduplication, asynchronous DNS with sequential IPv4 attempts, and staged six-RPC receiving with owning payloads. Clients are keyed by destination ID, with address changes, reconnection, and newest-three pending retention after failed connections. Distinguishes immediate submission errors from accepted completions. Close follows DNS/connect/write/timer/socket cleanup. Does not reproduce C's premature listener-memory release on errors or its multiple-address iteration bug.
- New checks: `raftpp.tcp_exchange`, `tcp_fragments`, `tcp_malformed`, `tcp_retry`, `tcp_lifetime`, `tcp_allocation`, `tcp_interop`, `tcp_cluster`, `tcp_failure`, and `tcp_shutdown`. Cover actual C TCP handshakes in both directions and RPC stream bytes; six message types/shared-payload lifetimes; fragmented/coalesced receiving; length/ID/protocol errors; failed-connect queues/address changes; destruction/exceptions inside callbacks; standard allocation failures 0..39 across four paths; native submission failures; and cancellation of actual partial writes during close.
- The actual disk-and-TCP three-node test stops a follower after eight commands and commits sixteen more on the majority. Both possible leaders must finish snapshot publication/compaction representing at least twenty commands before transfer and follower restart. Checks actual FSM restoration from a TCP-delivered snapshot, catch-up to twenty-four commands, the twenty-fifth command, full shutdown/restart, and the twenty-sixth command. This is not a mixed C-node cluster or power-cut test.
- Initial socket checks could not enter the transport path because of sandbox `EPERM`. Ran loopback checks outside that sandbox. Subsequently documented and configured the same `SIGPIPE` handling prerequisite as the C example/transport tests for writes to disconnected peers. Replaced a snapshot-count wait that allowed log-only catch-up with a wait for sufficiently recent snapshot compaction. Failed records were not reused as passes.
