### Tracing Unit: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: added `tracing.hpp`, `tracing.cpp`, and internal `detail/tracing.hpp`. Recursive reference scope covers C `tracing.c/.h` and public tracer declarations, event summaries in `raft.c`, errors/shutdown in `legacy.c`, election/conversion/receive/timeout, `uv_writer.c` submit/complete, and UV file/TCP call/lifetime boundaries. Product implementations use standard source locations, shared pointers, functions, atomics, chrono, and stdio.
- Preserves diagnostic code 1, levels 1..5, a 1023-byte message bound, writer submit 256/complete 512 with null data, no-op/stderr, and global enablement based on presence of `LIBRAFT_TRACE`. Default stderr is disabled; node construction enables it from the environment like C `raft_init`. Independent stack buffers replace C static formatting storage, and a standard atomic replaces the global switch.
- Shared callback ownership retains the active callable through replacement. Events/diagnostic data are synchronous borrows; consumers copy retained messages into standard strings. Disabled paths, copying, formatting, and emission allocate no library storage. Observer exceptions and nested tracing do not alter operation state. Observed-object operation reentry is prohibited; replacing its tracer is permitted.
- Added `tracing` to node/UV/TCP options and `set_tracer` to each class. Connected core events, role/term/vote/timeout/results, node error context, UV/TCP lifecycle/connections/retries/receives, and snapshot/segment completion diagnostics. UV setters propagate through coordinators to existing/future native writers. Node/UV/TCP channels are independent. Do not claim one-to-one reproduction of all detailed C messages or stable parsing compatibility for diagnostic text.
- Six new checks: `raftpp.tracing_callback`, `tracing_lifetime`, `tracing_stderr`, `core_tracing`, `writer_tracing`, and `tcp_tracing`. Cover format bounds, every level, source locations, environment behavior, custom events, replacement/exceptions/recursion, and allocation failure. Compare actual C formatting, selected core event summaries, and buffered/direct native writer event order. Tracing-enabled actual disk/TCP nodes exercise twenty-six commands, stopped-follower snapshot installation, transfer/full restart, node/UV/TCP tracer replacement, and paired writer metrics.
- The first stderr check failed because redirected FD and `FILE*` positions were not synchronized in the capture harness. Added `fseek` synchronization and reran only that check successfully. Strict compilation exposed omitted aggregate-member initialization warnings, fixed with explicit `tracer tracing{}` defaults. Failed results were not reused as passes. Original C sources are unchanged.

### Memory Resource Unit: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: added library resource get/set/reset and a standard Allocator adapter in `memory.hpp`/`memory.cpp`. Reference scope traces C `heap.c/.h` and public malloc/free/calloc/realloc/aligned/get/set/reset, buffer/entry/snapshot/codec/log, callback/node, and native disk/writer/log/file/TCP allocation/failure/cleanup. Replaces raw C heap ABI with standard PMR, zero-initialized buffers/resizing, standard `allocate_shared`, and PMR object construction.
- `resource_allocator<T>` supplements the standard polymorphic allocator with shared resource ownership and propagation traits. Does not implement control blocks or containers. `resource_owned<T>` aliases standard `unique_ptr` with a custom deleter; `allocate_owned` uses standard PMR `new_object/delete_object`. Connected existing library `make_shared/make_unique` construction paths to this allocator. Native resources such as libuv `addrinfo` retain their native deleters.
- Byte buffers use a standard vector with an explicit resource allocator; aligned-buffer deleters retain resource/requested bytes/alignment together. Move/swap propagate original allocators across unequal resources and preserve reusable moved-from objects. Shared payload control-block allocators retain resources until the last weak reference after payload destruction. The static default resource has no dynamic control block; its alias handle's weak/use_count semantics are documented.
- Selection uses a standard atomic shared pointer without changing other standard/external allocators. Ordinary vector/string/list backing, `std::function` boxing, filesystem/exception storage, and user/external-library allocations have separate boundaries. This unit does not complete allocator propagation through all internal containers. Future node requests use the selection at creation, rather than pinning it at node construction.
- Six new checks: `raftpp.memory_default`, `memory_alignment`, `memory_lifetime`, `memory_objects`, `memory_failure`, and `tcp_memory`. Cover C calloc/realloc zero/prefix comparisons, selection replacement/restoration, alignment/unequal-resource moves/swaps, resizing/construction/control-block failure, standard container rebinding, final weak release, and exact originating-resource/byte/alignment deallocation. A thread-safe tracking resource runs actual disk/TCP nodes, resetting selection after eight commands. After snapshot catch-up, transfer, twenty-six commands, full restart/shutdown, tracked allocation/deallocation counts match and the resource is destroyed. These counts are not a global leak proof across excluded allocator boundaries.
- The first `tracing_lifetime` failure showed that existing failure injection did not intercept standard PMR aligned new. Extended the test injector with standard aligned new/delete rather than hiding the failure in product code. Only affected checks were rechecked; they passed. The counter retains the same one-shot failure rule as ordinary new injection.

- Final standard-contract review corrected `allocate_shared<const T>` for entry/batch/tracer objects to use cv-unqualified `resource_allocator<T>`. Added const-object coverage and rechecked only six Debug checks (`memory_objects/entries/log/log_lifetime/tracing_lifetime/tcp_memory`) and three each in Release/ASan/UBSan (`memory_objects/tracing_lifetime/tcp_memory`); all passed. Reused other results whose input/behavior scopes were unchanged. Both consumers relinked against current libraries also passed. Logs: `/tmp/raftpp-memory-cv-debug.log`, `/tmp/raftpp-memory-cv-release.log`, `/tmp/raftpp-memory-cv-asan.log`.

### Native Container Unit: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: applied resource-aware standard containers to node request/operation/event/close-callback queues, writer request lists/buffer/iovec/io_event arrays, log_writer segment/prepare/truncate/barrier queues and temporary entry arrays, file_backend snapshot gets, uv_io sends, and TCP listener/peer/incoming/send queues/buffer descriptors/internal addresses. Preserved public value containers, file-list results, and native addrinfo deleters.
- Preserved allocator equality required by `splice`. TCP and segment pending/writing pairs and log-writer segments/retiring share allocators at construction. Every barrier-request group uses its parent barrier queue's allocator; temporary completion/cancellation lists take the source queue's allocator. Resource selection changes and reentrant close callbacks do not splice nodes with unequal allocators; creating cancellation lists allocates no storage.
- Reference scope covers C `uv_writer.c`, barrier/close paths in `uv_append.c`, and request ownership/queue cleanup in `uv_send.c`, `uv_tcp.c`, and `uv_snapshot.c`, together with callback/node/native-completion paths traced in preceding units. This unit changes the six C++ sources above, corresponding tests, bilingual memory APIs, and progress records. Consensus algorithms, disk/network formats, public API signatures, option dependency rules, and original C sources are unchanged.
- New UV check `raftpp.log_memory` creates the coordinator with A, submits append/barrier groups with B and C, then restores selection. It verifies normal completion/unblocking/durable order for two blocking groups and separately cancels both groups while the parent resource's next allocation is set to fail. Cancellation does not attempt that allocation and completes each callback exactly once. After final object destruction, checks weak expiration and matching allocation/deallocation counts/bytes/alignment for all three resources.
- The first selected ASan/UBSan run passed fourteen checks and timed out in `tcp_memory`. Its original output identified only the wait helper, so the failing phase cannot be determined. Added wait-call-site and node state/term/log/commit/applied/FSM/snapshot/error diagnostics without changing conditions or the twelve-second limit. That check then passed an isolated run in 8.03 seconds and investigation-only `--repeat until-fail:3` runs in 9.36/5.65/4.53 seconds. Preserve the initial failure; its cause remains undetermined. This does not prove that the timeout issue is resolved. Also rebuilt Debug/Release TCP targets after diagnostic changes; reused successful runtime results because success conditions/product execution paths are unchanged.

- 2026-11-02: inspected all of C `fixture.c` and public `fixture.h`, `test_fixture.c`, request copying/completion/cleanup, `LegacyFireCompletedRequests`, and node/FSM/log/random/snapshot integration. ### UV/TCP Settings: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: traced C settings in `uv.c`, bind settings in `uv_tcp.c`, per-operation allocation sizes in `uv_prepare.c`, size/capacity in `uv_append.c`, retry scheduling in `uv_send.c`, and C++ UV/file/log/TCP option/worker/callback/close boundaries. Added block/segment size, disk/connect retry, auto-recovery, and snapshot-compression settings to `uv_io`; bind/connect-retry settings to `tcp_transport`. Propagates through file/log coordinators without calling C. Original C sources are preserved.
- `transport::set_connect_retry_delay` is a default no-op virtual method preserving existing custom implementations. TCP uses the new delay for future scheduling without rearming existing timers; disk retries follow the same boundary. Compression setting is a successful no-op independently of LZ4, matching C. Bind replacement uses standard owning strings and preserves old values on failure; initialization freezes it with busy. Open-writer block-alignment changes return busy to preserve buffers/offsets. Does not reproduce unsafe intermediate alignment replacement or C's repeated-bind allocation leak.
- Segment-size changes affect existing append-fit/capacity policy and future preparations, preserving pools and in-flight writes/preparations. Each prepare captures its allocation size at first submission, retaining it through retries rather than reading mutable options from a worker. Only this capture path affects prior execution, so selected existing append/failure/close checks were rechecked.
- New `log_settings` occupies the single libuv worker to queue a preparation before changing size; verifies old 4096/new 1536-byte allocations, live retry after failed writing, close, and reload. `uv_io_settings` checks probe-block restoration, busy/invalid/shutdown, auto-recovery toggles with actual corrupt files, capacity propagation, and snapshot-preparation retry. `tcp_settings` checks actual bind connections, preserved addresses, repeated replacement/OOM, init freezing, advertised identity, and actual reconnection after a 60000→1 ms change.

### Per-Request Native Writer Diagnostics: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: traced C `uv_writer.c` request messages/result mapping/transfer, C++ KAIO/threadpool/poll/eventfd/init/shutdown, log retries/completions, file snapshot work, and UV protected callbacks. Added optional writer-initialization diagnostic output and internal writer/log/file `error_details()` queries. Product code uses existing standard-value error_info without delegating to C. Original C sources are preserved.
- Workers write only their own request status/diagnostic; the loop publishes it before completion. Preserves native errno, operation/FD/offset, and reported/expected write counts. Poll failures are copied to pending requests; shutdown work owns separate diagnostics that survive native handles. Existing codes, completion/buffer lifetimes, partial-write behavior, EAGAIN fallback, and cleanup order are preserved. Negative ENOSPC retains existing io_error status with ENOSPC cause.
- Logs add `open-N` names and causes. Successful retries may retain observed diagnostics without changing original results/last_error semantics. Snapshot work owns preparation/publication/retrieval context. Standard optional values prioritize the current completion's diagnosis, separating earlier fatal writer errors from new snapshot failures/cancellation. UV protected callbacks retain this information before calling users. Full per-syscall disk errno/path and TCP detail propagation remain outstanding.
- New `writer_diagnostics` covers five init/two immediate submission points, four concurrent workers with distinct lengths/offsets/causes, short writes/out-of-range synthetic results (INT64_MIN), shutdown queue EAGAIN retry, and actual NOWAIT/poll results. `log_diagnostics` checks successful retry after initial ENOSPC, retained cause/segment lifetime, and preserved success status. `file_diagnostics` checks four concurrent snapshot failures, partial writer-init causes, a different snapshot error after writer failure, retained successful-prepare-retry context, and real pending-prepare close/cancellation/cleanup.

### Per-call Disk Diagnostics: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: referenced file operations/error propagation in C `uv_fs.c`, `uv_metadata.c`, `uv_snapshot.c`, and `uv_segment.c`, plus C++ disk/probe/log_writer/file_backend/UV/node request and cleanup paths. Preserved `raft/`. Added optional `error_info*` outputs to error-returning disk_storage methods and probing; success clears them. Each call/worker owns its value without shared/TLS last-error storage. Separates paths/POSIX causes/existing Raft statuses and preserves initiating errors over best-effort cleanup.
- Captures native failures from metadata open/write/sync/close, file stat/read/scan/rename/unlink/allocate/truncate, O_TMPFILE/linkat snapshot publication, and probing. Expected absent metadata slots/unsupported capabilities and successful snapshot fallback leave empty outputs. Logical invalid/corrupt/allocation failures can have only operation/directory context with no native cause. This does not replicate every C message or verify every syscall-fault combination.
- Prepare/finalize/truncate requests own disk outputs; the loop publishes them before completion. Log callbacks prioritize the current request's diagnosis against reentrant failures. Snapshot workers retain causes while wrapping phase context. UV metadata calls update disk outputs before forwarding, avoiding argument-evaluation-order dependence. Existing callbacks/retries/cleanup/persistence ordering are preserved.
- New `disk_metadata_diagnostics` checks actual metadata1 EISDIR, injected write/close failures, filesystem_error EROFS, and independent-call cause reset. `disk_snapshot_diagnostics` checks successful corrupt-newest fallback, linkat EEXIST, four threads with sixteen independent outputs each, and actual snapshot-worker stat ENOENT. `disk_segment_diagnostics` checks O_EXCL EEXIST/finalize ENOENT, primary write failure over cleanup failure, OOM, and probe EACCES. `disk_node_diagnostics` replaces the running metadata2 path with a directory and verifies actual RequestVote open EISDIR through UV→node and owned diagnostics after shutdown. It uses no sockets.

### Per-request TCP Diagnostics: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: referenced causes/retries/eviction/write/read/close in C `uv_tcp.c`, `uv_tcp_connect.c`, `uv_tcp_listen.c`, `uv_send.c`, and `uv_recv.c`, plus C++ TCP/transport/UV/node request/error propagation. Preserved original C sources. Optional `transport::error_details()` defaults to empty; tcp_transport returns owning information. Custom overrides are not required; uses standard error_code/error_category/array/string_view.
- TCP libuv causes retain the `libuv` category and original negative values. Linux errno values map to standard error_condition; EAI/EOF/unknown retain libuv values. Synchronous listener resolution uses `getaddrinfo` or captured errno for EAI_SYSTEM. Category message lookup has the standard owning-string allocation boundary; diagnostic construction/query/copy allocate nothing. Includes operation/peer ID/configured address within the existing 256-byte bound.
- Connection attempts and sends own their information. Failed-connect eviction copies a frozen diagnosis into each completion, avoiding other-peer/reentrant contamination. Captures connection/handshake/RPC submission/completion, accept/read/decode, and callback-exception context. Cancellation creates its own peer context without an earlier cause. EOF/immediate-send diagnostics preserve existing last_error semantics. Preserves newest-three retention/retries/wire/handshake/completion/close ordering.
- UV copies transport init/start/immediate/asynchronous send failures before external callbacks. Missing/mismatched diagnoses fall back to the Raft status; a throwing custom transport does not reuse earlier context. Node failure policy is preserved; send failure does not necessarily shut down the node.
- New `tcp_diagnostics` checks listener ENOMEM/allocation-free copies/immediate write ENOBUFS/two peers' independent EIO/EPIPE completions/reentrant callback and owned-copy lifetimes. `tcp_peer_diagnostics` checks EAI_NONAME category/cancellation cause reset, actual two-peer ECONNREFUSED/one-of-four eviction/remaining cancellation, and actual EOF with preserved normal status. `tcp_backend_diagnostics` checks actual port collision through UV→node and after cleanup, UV immediate/asynchronous write causes, synchronous resolver category, and stale-context rejection for throwing custom transports. Write-completion injection waits for actual native completion and changes only the result using fixed test-only slots; no product injection code was added.

### Legacy Callback Compatibility Drivers: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: compared public `raft.h.in`/UV/fixture declarations, C legacy/convert/membership/fixture/log implementations and legacy integration tests, and C++ node/core/fixture/existing bridges. Started the full API-scope audit; this unit actually changes/verifies callback membership validation and barrier/command/configuration-change/transfer drivers. This does not complete every API combination. Product C++ remains independent; original `raft/` is preserved.
- After common lifecycle admission, add/remove validate IDs/addresses in a configuration copy before leadership/change availability. Assign checks leadership/transfer→role validity→change availability→target existence/current role. Previously, a pending change could return cannot_change before bad_role/duplicate_id/duplicate_address/bad_id. Success/persistence/response/callback contracts are preserved.
- New legacy_reference.c drives original C fixture/callback APIs with owning requests; legacy_compatibility.cpp drives the independent C++ fixture. Ordinary and basename unit-test executables explicitly set force_barrier in the driver; product code never inspects executable names. Compares each event's state/term/vote/last/commit/applied/timeout/leader, ordered membership/roles, persistence boundary/defined entry bytes, FSM application, and callback status/count/timing.
- legacy_barrier normal/forced compares election barrier policy, command/barrier ordering, and exclusion of barriers from FSM application. Legacy membership compares standby promotion, pending-change validation precedence, spare add/remove, and voter demotion. Legacy transfer compares command→leadership transfer→new-leader command and application at every FSM. C user raft_barrier allocates eight unspecified bytes: compare length/type/term/behavior, not those contents. Defined command/configuration bytes are compared.
- The first shared link failed because C hides logGet. Test targets compile unchanged C log.c into per-function sections and discard unused functions, retaining the reference-side lookup locally. Product C++ dependency/link rules are unchanged. Also corrected an initial assign local-variable-name collision before execution.
- **Reference C sanitizer failure:** N_MESSAGE_TYPES=6 at raft/src/fixture.c:30 defines arrays indexed by message type 6 (TIMEOUT_NOW), producing out-of-bounds n_send[6] at fixture.c:416. Both C transfer comparisons failed there under ASan/UBSan. Preserved failures/stacks in `/tmp/raftpp-legacy-asan.log`. Did not modify original C/relax sanitizer flags/rerun those two failures/reuse them as passes.
- Separate legacy_transfer_cpp normal/forced never constructs/calls the C fixture. Tests the same C++ scenario's command/barrier policy, exactly-once callbacks, new leader, and both commands applied at all three FSMs. Both passed ASan/UBSan. These are C++ request/lifetime checks, not sanitized C comparisons; C transfer's Debug/Release/core-only results remain separate.

### Native Server and Process-Cluster Examples: Implementation, Verification, and Reuse

- 2026-11-02: referenced FSM/periodic apply/snapshot/restore/transfer/signals/separate child stop-restart in original `raft/example/server.c`/cluster.c and traced current node/UV/TCP/error/cleanup contracts. Added independent public-C++-API examples/server.cpp, cluster.cpp, counter.hpp, runtime.hpp and CMake targets. Preserved original C; product library sources/headers are unchanged.
- Counter retains native eight-byte uint64_t commands/snapshots using memcpy/standard shared_ptr/containers/RAII buffers. Results share the counter value; snapshots own independent bytes. Server retains three voters/loopback base+ID/125ms apply/pre-vote/64-16 snapshot settings. Creates new directories, accepts ordinary existing-data bootstrap rejection, and recovers. Retains app/FSM/loop until callbacks/network/file handles actually drain on signal/duration shutdown.
- Supervisor uses libuv spawn/process/timer/signal and standard filesystem/strings/random. Retains random 1–15-second wait→SIGINT→exit/close→restart with existing data; finds the sibling executable independently of working directory. Adds bounded cycles/interval/base-port/FSM settings and final settling. Unexpected child failures stop/reap siblings; five-second forced-shutdown watchdog returns failure. Default data directory is /tmp/raftpp; existing data is not automatically deleted.
- Initial actual restart failed because the example treated node.error() as shutdown: cannot_bootstrap is a historical nonfatal diagnosis. Changed it to inspect unavailable state and reran only cluster. That run exposed indefinite handoff waiting during simultaneous shutdown, triggering supervisor watchdog failure. C legacy likewise waits for a new leader after becoming follower. Preserved library/consensus behavior; bounded only application handoff to two seconds, then node.close drains the actual request. Preserved both failures in `/tmp/raftpp-example-debug.log` and `/tmp/raftpp-example-debug-restart.log`; final cluster passed.
- RAFTPP_BUILD_EXAMPLES defaults OFF; ON without UV rejects configure. raftpp_example_server/raftpp_example_cluster link only C++; executable names are raftpp-example-server/raftpp-example-cluster. Tests/fixture/LZ4 are not required. Register new tests only with TESTS+EXAMPLES. Updated both AGENTS versions with the option/directory.
- New example_counter checks command/restore length validation/state preservation, independent snapshot bytes, and result ownership after FSM destruction. example_cluster checks three real children/one stop-restart/snapshot restore/four drained server records/all child reaping. example_failure covers help/invalid ID/bounded standalone follower exit, missing-executable partial process cleanup, and stopping siblings after second-server port collision. Tests use temporary data/available ports and kill only their owned process group on timeout.

- Reports use standard vector/map/algorithm/chrono/locale for exact nearest-rank p50/min/max nanosecond samples and Bencher-shaped JSON. They do not reproduce the C fixed-histogram clipping/bucket-median/minimum issues; throughput correctly uses 1,000,000,000 ns per second and fractional operation counts. Only the throughput helper was checked; submit reports actual apply/native-write latency. Rejects empty samples, invalid/duplicate/named metrics, nonfinite values, and durations; JSON numbers are independent of the global locale.
- Submit uses public C++ node/uv_io/tcp_transport and the actual disk writer, without original C delegation. Preserves first/subsequent payload framing budgets of 40/32 bytes and 10ms polling for two prepared files. Includes the first sample, but terminates a one-command run before waiting. Startup is outside measurements; snapshots do not occur within supported sizes. The default TCP address is `127.0.0.1:0` instead of the old fixed port. Requires matched writer submit/complete traces and real writer samples. Deadline/signal/startup/cleanup failures do not produce a successful measurement.
- Parsing uses from_chars with overflow checks. Buf is 48..1MiB and a multiple of eight; size preserves the C unsigned limit, at most UINT32_MAX, positive and divisible by 4096 and buf. Rejects small-buffer subtraction underflow, division by zero, and incomplete total sizes before execution. Owns only a private temporary path inside an existing directory, draining all requests/handles before deletion. Checks stdout flush after preparing JSON; `/dev/full` failure cannot return success.
- New checks: `raftpp.benchmark_report` (exact odd/even/zero/30ms singleton samples, throughput, errors, locale), `raftpp.benchmark_options` (defaults/short options/equals/size overflow/alignment/bounds/address/deadline), `raftpp.benchmark_submit` (actual 1/4/64-command runs, preparation wait, actual writer, startup failure, parent-file/temp-data cleanup), and `raftpp.benchmark_cli` (executable JSON parsing, invalid commands/arguments, stdout failure, cleanup). Register these four only with tests and benchmarks ON; preserve existing CTest registrations.

### Native Benchmark Disk/io_uring and Device Discovery Verification (2026-11-02)

- The preceding goal unit made progress by completing submit implementation/verification records; this unit implements the actual outstanding disk/io_uring scope. Referenced `raft/tools/benchmark/{disk.c,disk_uring.c,fs.c,fs.h,disk_options.h,disk_parse.c}` and the previously inspected report/timer/profiler call/resource/option boundaries. New independent C++ files are `tools/benchmark/{disk_options.hpp,disk.hpp,disk.cpp,uring.hpp,uring.cpp,device.hpp,device.cpp}`, connected through main/tool/test CMake and tests. Original C remains unchanged.
- C++20 provides no native FD/mmap/io_uring owner, so reused `detail::file_handle` and added a scoped ring owner. Reused aligned_buffer and standard number/path/container/statistics facilities. Added only the standard atomic_ref alias in common.hpp; product implementation/existing API/ABI/behavior are unchanged. Existing Linux-header detection drives target-local RAFTPP_BENCHMARK_HAS_IO_URING; without headers, submit remains available and disk returns an explicit unsupported error. No liburing or other dependency was added.
- Actual Linux syscalls own/map SQ/CQ/SQEs and register fixed files/buffers for WRITE_FIXED/RWF_DSYNC. Supports both SINGLE_MMAP and separate SQ/CQ mappings, sequential depth-one writes, and CQE byte-count/negative-error/token checks. Uses lock-free standard atomic_ref acquire/release and validates ring sizes/offsets/alignment/masks. Handles SQ/CQ counter wrap, EINTR before/after consumption, retracting unaccepted failed submissions, and partial-construction cleanup. Deadline/signal failure drains accepted I/O before unregister/munmap/close; close alone is not assumed to complete asynchronous kernel teardown. Draining a failed device can exceed the deadline.
- Directories receive only private temporary files, preallocated/prefilled with aligned zeros and file/directory-fsynced before measurement. Actual write buffers use C's i%128 pattern. Raw block-device paths query capacity and overwrite the first size bytes without preallocation/truncation/removal. Rejects direct regular-file targets, excessive sizes, zero/misaligned input. Sysfs discovery uses standard paths/streams/from_chars, initialized generic fallback for unregistered filesystems such as overlay, 64-bit sector-range overflow checks, parent write-cache discovery for NVMe partitions, and nullb devices. Exact samples replace device-specific histogram resolution.
- A warm-up at offset zero is excluded from individual samples, followed by exactly size/buf measured writes. Throughput preserves fractional MiB and uses real seconds including ring setup/warm-up/measured loop/ring teardown. Reports actual success only, with one disk:<buf> entry containing latency (p50/min/max ns) and throughput (MiB/s). API docs/CLI explain raw-device overwrites and unsupported/deadline/cleanup contracts. No alternate backend is labeled as io_uring.
- Five new benchmark_disk_options/device/target/ring/native scopes cover parsing/overflow, synthetic sysfs 64-bit sectors/cache/malformed inputs, real O_DIRECT files/initialization/parent preservation/FD/temp cleanup, injected-syscall ring/failure/lifetime behavior, and actual kernel writes. Ring simulations cover shared/separate mmap, counter wrap, fixed SQEs/patterns, registration/mapping failures, CQE errors/short/invalid results, unaccepted retraction, and deadline/signal draining. Only unavailable native execution returns 77 as a distinct skip. Extended the existing CLI scope with actual 512-byte disk JSON, invalid-option handling, and unsupported-output contracts.

### Kernel Perf/Trace Implementation and Selected Verification (2026-11-02)

- The preceding disk/io_uring unit made progress through completed implementation/verification records. Rechecked complete `raft/tools/benchmark/profiler.c/.h` and disk/options/device/cleanup calls. New independent files are `tools/benchmark/{profiler.hpp,profiler.cpp,profiler_data.hpp,profiler_data.cpp}`, connected to disk_options/disk/main/device/CMake/tests. Original C, library sources/public API/ABI/common headers are unchanged. Consulted official Linux perf ABI/tracepoint/event-tracing material.
- Uses tracepoint format scalar/array/__data_loc offsets/sizes rather than fixed C raw structs, memcpy-based native scalars, and little-endian NVMe command words. Writable mmap uses head-acquire/tail-release and checks wrapped/incomplete/lost/overflow/throttled records. Standard vectors/maps/algorithms own records and correlate across CPUs by monotonic time. No standard containers/smart pointers/task-promises were reimplemented.
- Supports noncontiguous online CPUs. Each per-CPU perf group owns 64 sampling pages plus one metadata page and redirects child output to the leader with SET_OUTPUT ioctl, without depending on FD_OUTPUT. Handles FD/mmap/partial initialization/enable failures/stop/reads/scheduling multiplexing/explicit-close errors; destructors clean remaining resources. Consumes rings after each write and at stop; reports only complete command correlations.
- Distinguishes block device/parent numbers/partition offsets and NVMe namespace/logical-block size/controller-queue-command keys. Supports CID reuse and duplicate rq/bio completions without mixing unrelated completions into tracked commands. Independent samples produce disk:block:<buf> and disk:nvme:<buf>; does not reproduce C's NVMe report reuse of block data. Missing/ambiguous/malformed/failed/split-merged commands or voluntary context switches fail measurement; generic/unidentified devices do not emit empty successful reports. Actual NVMe/nullb workloads remain unverified.
- Added --perf/-p, repeatable --trace/-t, and --tracefs. Validates names/duplicates/path traversal and supports real hyphenated names such as xhci-hcd. Trace sessions preserve/enable/restore each event-enable and tracing_on, retaining mixed states. They do not clear trace contents; records remain in the selected mount's trace, as in C. Exceptions/partial starts also attempt restoration. Tests modify only private tracefs fixture files, never host controls. Perf header detection is a nonrequired UV configure check and target-local HAS_PERF definition.
- Registered seven benchmark_profiler_options/format/correlation/trace/ring/perf/native scopes. The first six cover options/malformed formats/dynamic fields/noncontiguous CPUs/partition-4KiB-LBA-CID reuse/independent latencies/mixed trace restoration/partial failures/wrapped-lost records/injected-syscall initialization-FD-mmap-enable-multiplexing and collector-to-JSON. Native checks only actual format access/disabled perf FD-mmap initialization and never enables host events. It does not establish hardware-metric verification.

### Raw-Stream Ownership and TCP Handoff Verification (2026-11-02)

- The preceding goal work made progress by updating test-reuse instructions and bilingual record formats. Continued by checking actual raw-stream sources and terminal build status, tracing raft/src/uv_tcp{,_connect,_listen}.c, uv_send.c, uv_recv.c, public UV-transport members, and C++ TCP/codec/error/memory/callback cleanup. Original C sources are unchanged.
- Added public uv_stream.hpp/uv_stream_transport.hpp, independent uv_stream.cpp, existing-TCP raw branches, and shared private uv_error.hpp. RAFTPP_WITH_UV gates sources/header declarations/six new CTests. Fixture and LZ4 are independent; the C++ product calls no reference C implementation.
- Standard owning pointers/functions and resource standard containers retain each native handle's original allocation. Adoption transfers ownership only on constructor success; native data is restored before invoking the deleter after close. Own read chunks/scatter-write buffers; preserve accepted-write completion → native close/deleter → close observers. Additional close-observer allocation failure is explicit; no-argument close/destruction starts closing without allocation.
- Raw TCP can connect without listening and completes once after trying resolved IPv4 addresses, separately from message-mode indefinite connection retry. Handed streams remain independent after connector close/actual destruction. Stop handshake reading before incoming handoff and never move native requests/handles. uv_stream declares TCP/pipe/TTY adoption; this unit's native execution verifies TCP only.

### Raw-Plugin Message Adapter and UV Integration Verification (2026-11-02)

- The preceding goal unit made progress implementing/verifying raw-stream owners/TCP handoff. This unit traced original raft/src/uv_send.c clients/pending queues/eviction/retries/address changes/write-close and uv_recv.c receive stages, plus C++ codec/uv_stream/transport/uv_io/node callback/ownership boundaries. Original C sources, the existing TCP message engine, and disk/consensus algorithms are unchanged.
- New stream_transport.hpp/stream_transport.cpp exclusively manage a raw connector lifecycle and provide message framing/send queues/reconnection. Shared state, resource standard containers, and standard reference callables own state/messages through actual completion. Immediate connect errors preserve the queue; accepted failed connects retain only the newest three. Address changes/full close cancel pending requests. Reconnect only after failed-stream native close; preserve completion/deleter/close-callback ordering.
- Store the first nonempty uv_stream close observer by moving its standard function into a member, requiring no additional allocation; only later observers need list nodes. Adapter observers use std::ref, which also avoids callable-target allocation. Failed incoming admissions are counted separately and wait for native deleter completion before overall close. UV gates the new API/source/seven CTests; fixture/LZ4 are not required.
- Tests use an independent pipe plugin, inline connect/accept, and stalled connects in addition to TCP. Verified all six bidirectional RPC byte formats with 3/7-byte read fragments, existing TCP message-engine interoperability, newest-three queue retention/immediate-submit retry/address changes/allocation failures/native EOF/oversized frames/callback exceptions/8MiB pending-write cancellation and ownership, and actual-disk three-node elections/four replicated commands/close.

### Optional Assertion-Backtrace Port and Verification (2026-11-03)

- The preceding turn made progress by clarifying test-record preservation/reuse rules. This unit compared original configure.ac options and Makefile.am source/link rules, finding that optional backtraces from src/assert.h were missing in C++. Original C sources remain unchanged. This is concrete evidence that a public-function inventory alone cannot establish complete functionality.
- RAFTPP_WITH_BACKTRACE defaults OFF. ON checks GNU libbacktrace headers/library and the original __assert_fail requirement at configure time. Exposes a generated 0/1 macro and features::backtrace. Independent of UV/LZ4/fixture; OFF does not find/link this dependency. Static consumers inherit required linkage. Probe results distinguish dependency file hashes/paths/toolchain/compilation-link inputs, avoiding success reuse for a different library and reusing an earlier result when its original inputs return.

The dependency may allocate its own memory. Collection failure still aborts; no global signal handler, exception, or custom tracer is introduced.
- New raftpp.assertions triggers the actual library's minimum<=maximum assertion in random_within_range inside a child process. Checks library code/across a DSO: actual location/expression/frames when ON, standard abort when OFF, and NDEBUG disabling. The NDEBUG input takes an explicit UINT32_MAX branch, avoiding division/overflow UB. The child disables core dumps. Preserved existing CTest registrations and added one new check.

### Native-Capacity Arithmetic and No-Space Admission Compatibility Correction (2026-11-03)

- The preceding turn made progress by porting/verifying original optional assertion backtraces. This unit referenced original uv_ip.c/uv_tcp*.c IPv4 resolution/multiple-address attempts/listen-handshake-completion-close, uv_send.c/uv_recv.c, then uv.c/uv_prepare.c/uv_append.c capacity production through client.c/core consumption. Did not infer IPv6 as an original feature; network implementations are unchanged.
- Actual product changes are two capacity calculations in src/uv_io.cpp and src/log_writer.cpp. Original uvUpdateCapacity adds prepared-file count times configured segment size and UvAppendCapacity in size_t, then converts KiB to unsigned short. C++ size_t/uint16_t saturation produces different reports and changes consensus no-space admission. Preserve defined unsigned-size_t wraparound/addition and uint16_t conversion. Introduces no signed overflow; allocation geometry, append-fit/reservation, serialization, state-transition algorithms, and callback contracts are unchanged.
- New tests/capacity_reference.c reads io.capacity from actual raft_uv_init/io.init/start/append and loop prepare/check; it does not copy original arithmetic. Its fake transport opens no sockets. Stabilizes two prepared files alone and one active segment containing an eight-byte command plus the prepared pool at 4 KiB. Samples twelve reporting sizes in each state, yielding 24 actual C values: 65534/65535/0/1/2 boundaries, active reserved bytes, and size_t accumulation near int64 limits. Retains existing small files instead of allocating enormous files.
- New uv_io_capacity_reference collects both implementations' actual results and closes normally before comparing them. Reproduced C=0/C++=65535 and large active-sum discrepancies before correction; preserves the initial failure separately. After correction all 24 C/C++ samples match, C++ files remain at most 4096 bytes, and shutdown capacity is zero. New uv_io_capacity_admission checks immediate no_space/no completion/log-FSM preservation/healthy node for a high threshold on an actual-disk single voter, then one commit/apply and native cleanup after restoring normal geometry/threshold.

### Local Snapshot Preparation Allocation Failure: Node Survival and Retry (2026-11-03)

Reference scope: original legacy.c legacyShouldTakeSnapshot/legacyTakeSnapshot/putSnapshot/takeSnapshotClose/takeSnapshotCb/legacyHandleEvent, configuration.c configurationCopy/configurationAdd, public FSM/heap/tracer/fixture, and C++ node.cpp apply_committed/process/maybe_snapshot/launch/pump/install. The original cleans up request malloc/configuration-address malloc/server realloc failures through abort and waits for a later event. Equivalent optional-work exceptions in C++ reached generic process failure handling and closed the node despite an already applied command.

Before the fix, new raftpp.node_snapshot_allocation failed its node-survival check at the first allocation. Preserved `/tmp/raftpp-snapshot-allocation-before-build.log` and `-before.log`. src/node.cpp maybe_snapshot now calls the separated existing take_snapshot body and absorbs only bad_alloc/length_error in that scope. After-freeze failures use existing submission exception cleanup to restore taking_snapshot and finalize the FSM. Other first-phase exceptions and accepted-storage completion errors retain their closing contract. No changes to original raft/, public signatures/generated headers, or product link requirements.

The new C++ check sweeps 32 allocation positions immediately after apply. Long (128-byte) configuration/event address copies, FSM buffers, asynchronous requests/callbacks/queues/immediate submission yield 13 skipped attempts (six finalized exactly once after freezing) and 19 submissions. Each checks commit/applied=2, one successful command callback, stored log/node survival, snapshot index=2/configuration index=1/ID-address-role after retry, and a subsequent command at applied=3. Also distinguishes length_error skip/retry from arbitrary FSM exceptions that close the node. Existing node_snapshot/node_restore/node_allocation cover pending installation/restore/second FSM phase/immediate-completion failures/cancellation/general allocation contracts.

New C11 helper tests/snapshot_allocation_reference.c applies a command on an actual single-voter C fixture and fails each of three request/address/server allocations immediately after the original take-snapshot trace. All real memory uses the previous heap for allocation/free; the heap is restored before closing. Each state/commit/applied/commands/failures/prepared/finalized/retry-state tuple matches `[3,2,2,1,1,0,0,3,1,1]`. This does not claim identical event timing for C/C++ snapshot attempts. The C helper/reference link is included only with fixture ON and excluded with fixture OFF.

Source inspection also found the original takeSnapshotCb pending-install branch clears snapshot_pending and then reads it again. This C comparison stays within single-node preparation failures and does not execute that branch. Existing C++ node_snapshot checks pass concurrent local snapshot/installation, but runtime comparison of that original C branch remains unverified.

### Fixture Snapshot Injection/Load: Preserve the Suffix (2026-11-03)

The previous goal unit made progress by implementing/verifying optional local snapshot allocation-failure skip/retry. This unit traced manual fixture snapshot/entry injection from the public-functionality inventory. Reference scope: original fixture.c ioMethodLoad/raft_fixture_set_snapshot/raft_fixture_add_entry/ioFlushSnapshotPut/ioMethodTruncate/ioClose/initialization-startup, legacy.c startup/FSM restore, entry.c/snapshot.c deep copies, C++ fixture.cpp copy_state/set_snapshot/add_entry/complete(put_job)/load/persisted, and node startup. Original ioMethodLoad copies all stored entries and returns snapshot.index+1 as their start. C++ copy_state filtered against the previous stored start_index, potentially removing a suffix injected before the snapshot or entries retained by nonzero-trailing storage.

Before the fix, new fixture_snapshot_load_reference failed suffix preservation. Preserved `/tmp/raftpp-fixture-snapshot-load-before-build.log` and `-before.log`. Product changes are limited to the snapshot-load branch of src/fixture.cpp copy_state. Deep-copy every stored entry and return it as a suffix starting at snapshot.index+1; preserve max-index overflow rejection, unchanged output on failure, and raw stored-state copying by persisted. Actual file-backend prefix handling, consensus algorithms, serialization formats, public signatures, and product CMake/linking are unchanged. Original raft/ is preserved.

The new check compares 36 cases: snapshot indices 2/5/10/64, zero/one/three entries, and injection before/after/on both sides of snapshot injection. Each runs two repeated loads and one load after node startup, comparing term/vote/start/count, snapshot term/index/configuration index, membership ID-role-address, snapshot/FSM/entry bytes and order. Public startup state/term/last-commit-applied and FSM restore/command counts also match. Nine separate cases compare actual queued snapshot-put event/completion and two loads for zero/one/three entries and trailing 0/1/4. Total: 45 cases and 126 C-load comparisons. One input with a later snapshot index and three entries sweeps 24 standard-allocation positions, checking OOM output preservation and successful copying.

Added injection/load/start helpers in tests/fixture_reference.c. Existing creation preserves node-start/raw-I/O-start flows while permitting a new unstarted configuration. The existing FSM restore is unchanged; only the new injected-start helper restores a valid eight-byte state. C-returned snapshot/configuration/buffers/batches and fixture-owned injected data use their respective cleanup paths. C-load allocation failure follows original assertions and is outside the C runtime comparison; C++ error-return/output-preservation checks are separate.

### Actual-C Event-Core Leadership Transfer Comparison (2026-11-03)

The previous goal unit made progress fixing/verifying fixture snapshot injection/load losing suffix entries. Tracing storage/replication index boundaries in this unit exposed the relationship between replicationUpdate partial ACKs and transfer startup, refining scope to transfer verification. References: original client.c ClientTransfer/clientSelectTransferee, replication.c replicationUpdate, membership.c membershipLeadershipTransferStart, recv_timeout_now.c/recv_request_vote.c, timeout.c/convert.c; C++ client/replication/membership/receive_core/receive/timeout/convert and public core/node queries/docs. Referencing these sources does not establish verification of the entire index domain.

Both original and C++ can start TimeoutNow on an ACK for new partial replication progress. The RPC advertises the leader's full log end; a recipient with a different durable boundary ignores it. Once marked transferring, later ACKs alone do not resend it, so early delivery can lead to expiration and an explicit new request. Did not arbitrarily change this original algorithm. The earlier core API description requiring a concrete transfer_event ID contradicted supported ID-zero automatic selection; corrected both languages.

New raftpp.core_transfer compares actual raft_step through the existing oracle with C++ step across nine cases/135 events. Every event checks state/term-vote/commit-stored/all log terms/configurations/clock-random-timeout/progress-catch-up-transfer fields/update flags/RPC order-fields/persistence ranges and entry bytes. Three automatic-selection cases choose caught-up voter two/three or last voter three when none is caught up, rejecting self/unknown/standby/spare targets. Duplicate transfer requests return not_leader before target validation. Four transfer cases combine pre_vote OFF/ON and early/delayed hints with max_inflight_entries=1 and five-command partial replication. Verified ignored early hints/no resends on later ACKs/expiration with quorum/explicit retry; delayed hints trigger disruptive elections, step down the old leader on a higher term, and elect the recipient. Also compared single-voter not_found and follower/candidate not_leader.

No C fixture calls occur, avoiding its known original TIMEOUT_NOW statistics-array defect. Compared the actual C transfer core with fixture OFF under ASan/UBSan too. This neither fixes C legacy-fixture sanitizer failures nor revalidates callback completion order. Reused prior node callback/shutdown records. Product sources/public headers/original raft/, feature options, and product link requirements are unchanged.

The initial new-test build lacked the public query header; added core.hpp to tests/core.cpp (diagnostics preserved in tool output; the initial file log was replaced by a subsequent build). The first new Debug runtime passed. Strict Release then reported GCC 13 maybe-uninitialized on a redundant temporary variant move in existing core_cluster. Preserved `/tmp/raftpp-core-transfer-release-initial-build.log`. Changed the cluster send lambda to std::forward concrete events, removing unnecessary variant construction without weakening warning flags. Added only that existing test to the affected scope. The new transfer check is unaffected, so reused its Debug result instead of rerunning it.

### Actual-C Native File Reservation Fallback Comparison (2026-11-03)

The previous goal unit made progress with actual-C event-core transfer comparison and API description corrections. Auditing configure.ac/Makefile.am options and native I/O found EOPNOTSUPP reservation emulation implemented but without recorded execution comparison, refining scope to that path. References: original uv_fs.c UvFsAllocateFile/uvFsAllocate/error cleanup, uv_os.c UvOsFallocate/uvOsFallocateEmulation, C++ file.cpp allocate/open/close and disk.cpp prepare_segment/allocate/diagnostic propagation. C++20 has no equivalent space-reservation facility, so retained existing POSIX/libuv dependencies. Product sources/original raft/public headers/feature options/link requirements are unchanged.

New raftpp.file_allocation_reference compares actual C UvFsAllocateFile with C++ disk_storage::prepare_segment. A test-only C11 interposer traces only the reservation fd explicitly armed in thread-local state; unarmed calls reach libc. Different posix_fallocate return/errno values check that the actual return determines failure. Injected fstatfs block sizes/errors still perform successful fallback pwrite calls on actual new files. Provides ordinary/64-bit libc entry points, avoiding duplicate definitions when 64-bit names are macro aliases. C references reuse isolated probe_reference_fs/os OBJECTs without altering other tests/product source properties or syscalls. The new target is UV-only and does not link the complete original C library.

Fallback success covers 54 combinations: block sizes 0/512/1024/2048/4096/8192 and sizes 1/511/512/513/4095/4096/4097/8192/8193. Add one native success, four native EIO/ENOMEM/EINVAL/EACCES failures, three fallback-stat EIO/ENOMEM/EPERM failures, and twelve fallback-pwrite failures (EIO/ENOSPC/EINVAL/EINTR at write zero/one/three): 74 cases. Status/allocation-stat-write counts/all write offsets match C. Successful files have exact sizes/all-zero bytes; fallback checks intervals capped at 4096 and the final byte. Failures unlink created files and close C descriptors while preserving the C++ caller's existing owning /dev/null output handle. Owning C++ diagnostics retain actual native causes/open-1 paths; success clears old diagnostics. Each case also checks the final descriptor count.

The original wraps fallback stat/write errors as EOPNOTSUPP, ultimately io_error, so fallback ENOSPC is io_error too. C++ preserves that status while retaining the ENOSPC cause. Reused prior disk_no_space verification of native posix_fallocate ENOSPC/no_space without rerunning it. This tests injected branches and actual file writes, not an actual musl execution or filesystem returning EOPNOTSUPP.

### C Comparisons for Wire-Length Declarations and Staged Payload Boundaries (2026-11-03)

The preceding goal turn was progress: it actually changed and verified election-term/clock guards, tests, APIs, and records. Preserved existing encoding_malformed records for all truncations/corruption, 5000 random inputs, and configured-limit errors, plus eight-path allocation failures in encoding_failure. The new check does not rerun these merely to obtain their results; it adds width-boundary/declared-length/staged-assembly inputs absent from those records. It records large numbers in headers of a few dozen bytes rather than constructing nonexistent memory spans or multi-GiB buffers.

The new encoding_limits has 25 cases on this 64-bit host: (1) eight batches containing zero/UINT32_MAX/UINT32_MAX-7/two maximum lengths/mixed zero lengths, inclusive header/count/payload limits and one-unit-over encode/decode/RPC output preservation; (2) individual UINT32_MAX+1 rejection; (3) hostile 2^32/UINT64_MAX counts; (4) zero/INT64_MAX/UINT64_MAX header declarations and full-frame bounds; (5) zero/one/UINT64_MAX snapshot data lengths; (6) mixed zero/eight-byte entry assembly/shared lifetime and seven inconsistent staged headers. Full-message bounds use subtraction, preserving output/consumed instead of wrapping when adding preamble/header to a large length. Weak references check the final owner release for this particular batch, not whole-heap leak freedom. Small-size_t branches exist in the test code, but no actual 32-bit build ran.

UV configurations directly call unchanged C uvEncodeBatchHeader/uvDecodeBatchHeader/uvDecodeMessage. Twenty-four byte/field/RPC-total checks for eight batches and three snapshot-length checks match: 27 valid comparisons total. No payload bytes are read or allocated. One separate check observes the original uvDecodeBatchHeader 64-to-unsigned truncation: wire count 2^32 becomes C n=0/success, while C++ returns too_big and preserves output/consumed. That specific value is safe because C performs no further allocation/entry reads. Do not execute C allocation/reads for UINT64_MAX or other hostile counts. Malformed-input C behavior is not blindly reproduced, and the original raft/ tree is unchanged.

The pipe check executes no AF_INET sockets or actual TCP cluster.

New raftpp.stream_pipe creates two real native Linux pipe2/uv_pipe_init-open handles and adopts them through public uv_stream in tests/uv_stream.cpp. Five cases cover (1) three-byte read chunks/empty middle scatter/moved writer/buffer ownership-release through completion/chunks surviving native close; (2) canceling an actual pending write larger than F_GETPIPE_SZ once, with retained payload and native-deleter/two-close-observer ordering; (3) closing the writer to generate actual EOF, retaining UV_EOF, terminating subscription, and closing reentrantly once; (4) active-pipe adoption rejection preserving existing data/ownership/deleter; (5) immediate read-only-end write rejection without completion, preserving the native cause. Original native data is restored before release, each allocation is released once, and the loop drains to closure. This does not establish whole-heap leak freedom, TTY execution, or message-adapter framing over a real pipe connector.

### Native-TTY Stream Contract Verification with Private PTYs (2026-11-03)

The preceding goal turn was progress, adding five actual-pipe cases and API/progress records. This unit checked AGENTS, both status documents, uv_stream TCP/pipe/TTY adoption/shared writes-read chunks/move-EOF-close implementation, prior raw-stream/pipe verification, and APIs. Actual PTYs verify a public contract lacking native-TTY execution evidence. Edits are tests/uv_stream.cpp, one UV-conditional CTest registration, and paired uv_stream APIs/status. Product code, public declarations, feature dependencies, consensus algorithms, formats, and reference raft/ are unchanged.

First ran the independent C11/libuv experiment /tmp/raftpp-tty-native-probe.c with a new PTY, establishing raw-byte read/write and the native result after master close. Output showed original_fd=4/native_fd=11 ownership duplication, `received=4 written=1 master_read=5 ended=-4095 (end of file)`, and `loop_close=0`. Descriptor numbers are environment observations, not test conditions. This establishes actual uv_tty_t/current-libuv behavior, not comparison against a separate cowsql/raft TTY implementation.

New stream_tty only uses private master/slave pairs created by posix_openpt/grantpt/unlockpt/ptsname_r. O_NOCTTY avoids acquiring a controlling terminal; existing stdin/stdout/user terminals and global uv_tty_set_mode/reset_mode state are untouched. Raw termios/window-size changes apply only to the newly created slave. When libuv duplicates its descriptor, separate RAII closes the original slave fd; adopted native allocation/duplicated fd remain owned through asynchronous close. Adoption-constructor exceptions schedule uv_close cleanup of the still-owned native allocation.

Five actual cases cover (1) UV_TTY type/73×21 window-size query/bidirectional scatter bytes including NUL-CR-LF/two-byte read chunks/write ownership-release through completion/read chunks surviving close; (2) subscription stop/master-buffered data/no callback/subscription restart and byte delivery; (3) master-close UV_EOF cause/restart rejection/reentrant EOF close with exactly one deleter-observer sequence; (4) active-TTY adoption rejection preserving data/existing ownership; (5) move assignment between two TTYs on the same loop, closing the old native allocation, preserving the incoming address, bidirectional I/O through the new owner, and original-deleter data restoration. All conditions execute in Release. These owner/allocation observations concern the test resources, not whole-heap leak freedom.

The 2026-11-02 “Raw-Plugin Message Adapter and UV Integration Verification” record and current source already contain a real pipe connector adopting AF_UNIX socketpairs as uv_pipe_t. adapter_exchange/retry/close/receive_failure/allocations/uv_io use actual streams/kernel I/O, including a native-disk three-node election/four-command replication/shutdown. Therefore the recent plain pipe/TTY units incorrectly summarized pipe-connector framing as unverified. Do not report those old pipe checks as newly passing or rerun them to recreate logs. TTY connectors were outside that earlier verification and are this unit's new scope.

Independent C11/libuv experiment /tmp/raftpp-tty-adapter-native-probe.c initializes a private PTY master as uv_pipe_t and slave as uv_tty_t, configures nonblocking writes on both, then closes them. Observed `master_type=7 slave_type=14 nonblocking=0/0` and `loop_close=0`. The new test factory applies raw termios only to that new slave, without changing controlling/existing terminals or global TTY modes. Native deleters own shared statistics rather than borrowing connector state. Default bus tty=false preserves existing pipe paths/fault injection/native calls; only new tty=true cases select the factory/direction swap.

New adapter_tty has three cases. First, two adapters use an application connector with inline accept/connect and swap master-pipe/slave-TTY send directions by ID. Three/five-byte read chunks carry all six RPC types bidirectionally: 12 complete messages, comparing bytes/order/sender IDs-addresses/request ownership through completion/final release and draining both pairs of native deleters before close. Second, send a four-MiB AppendEntries over TTY to an unread peer, verify actual write_queue_size>0, then destroy adapter/connector wrappers. The accepted request completes canceled exactly once while its message remains alive; native TTY release precedes adapter close observation. Close the independent peer afterward to release both allocations. This does not promise withdrawal of an already written prefix. Third, write only eight preamble bytes and close the PTY master, preserving actual TTY UV_EOF through the adapter, delivering no incomplete message, and releasing both native allocations.

New asynchronous test callbacks own shared result state, avoiding test-local-reference UAF during wrapper destruction/failure-loop draining. These ownership/allocation observations concern the selected resources, not whole-heap leak freedom. The 12 are message transfers, not 12 commands in a new TTY-based Raft/disk cluster. Distinguish earlier actual pipe/native-disk integration from this TTY message boundary.

The concrete verification gap closed here was compressed-snapshot integration with actual transport, still listed in the feature table. Traced src/disk.cpp load_snapshot/decompression, file_backend worker snapshot_get, node needs_data lookup→owning payload send and receiver installation→snapshot_put, TCP framing and node recovery. Product sources/public signatures/formats/reference raft/ were unchanged. Added tests/compressed_transfer.cpp and one UV-conditional target/CTest.

The fixed input is a 46-byte frame generated by the existing liblz4 LZ4F_compressFrame(default preferences). It contains a little-endian count=41 and 4088 bytes of 0xa5, totaling 4096 bytes. Keeping the fixture bytes in source lets LZ4-OFF use the same valid input without requiring a test compressor dependency. Seed two disk nodes with this term=3/index=20/configuration_index=1 snapshot, start a three-voter majority and commit the first command to count=42. Start a third node late with only an index-1 bootstrap and verify actual TCP snapshot installation/command catch-up. The receiver's actual index-20 snapshot file contains exactly the 4096 decoded bytes. Restart all nodes and commit a second command to count=43 everywhere. Both sender files retain all 46 original compressed bytes across shutdown/restart. With LZ4 OFF, both starts return invalid, FSM restore runs zero times, and shutdown/source preservation are checked.

Initial Debug/shared Release ON/OFF checks passed before comparing receiver file bytes directly. Preserved /tmp/raftpp-compressed-transfer-{debug,release-on,release-off}-initial.log. Added actual-file comparison so a decompressed return buffer alone does not claim uncompressed on-disk storage, then rebuilt/reran only the changed check. The ASan configuration first ran the final input. This is neither a product defect fix nor evidence establishing the original TCP timeout cause.

Replace intrusive queue ABI with standard list/deque and owning requests rather than reimplementing queue macros. Raw heap ABI similarly maps to standard PMR/buffer ownership-resize-alignment contracts. Audit remaining individual contracts by distinguishing standard replacements, actual equivalent checks and reference/environmental limits. References this unit covered the four original fuzzy files, lib/legacy randomize-kill-add-assign-progress helpers, fixture/node/consensus receive-conversion paths, original RequestVoteResult and public union layout. Actual changes are tests/fuzzy.cpp/fuzzy_reference.c/stale_vote.cpp, test CMake, src/receive_core.cpp, paired core/node APIs and this record. Original raft/ remains unchanged.

The fuzzy ports use fixed seeds 85213/70011/0x3914306f. Election covers 3/4/5/7 nodes×pre_vote OFF/ON×four scenarios; replication 3/5/7 nodes×four scenarios; membership 3/4/5 nodes×adding standby/adding voter/leader self-removal; liveness 3/4 nodes×pre_vote OFF/ON. Preserve disk latency=10 for completion ordering, varying network latency 25–50/election timeout 1000–2000. Pairwise disruptions use 10% probability, 50–150 event duration and at most 25000 events. New targets require only FIXTURE and are independent of UV. Results below match for the same inputs across configurations; summing configurations does not create new scenarios.

| Scenario group | Selected scenarios | C++ fixture events | Valid C event comparisons | Scenarios stopping C comparison |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| election | 96 | 61209 | 50366 | 60 |
| replication | 36 | 20901 | 2836 | 36 |
| membership | 27 | 6230 | 2135 | 25 |
| liveness | 12 | 302737 | 149350 | 12 |
| Total | 171 | 391077 | 204687 | 133 |

C comparisons check event index/type/time and each node's state/term/last_index/commit/applied/FSM plus stable leader. C++ properties also check at-most-once request completion, minority election/commit restrictions, leader loss/outdated-node recovery, membership propagation and fresh commits after self-removal. The oracle was a test-only C adapter. C++ events after oracle shutdown are not counted as matching C comparisons.

Initial experiments identified three harness assumptions. Fixed election timeouts caused the original C implementation to repeat split votes through term 19 for n=4/seed=0x3914306f. Refresh timeouts at timer-reset ticks as the original does; after C comparison ends, continue independent C++ execution with tick refreshes in the same valid range. Commands submitted to a cached leader immediately after reconnect can return leadership_lost in both implementations, so observe changed links and wait for live-node terms/roles to align. The original partitioned scenario cuts leader-to-majority edges while allowing other peers to communicate. Actual C also let a successor commit an earlier accepted command for n=7/seed=0x3914306f. Replace the global count=0 cancellation assumption with no own-term minority-leader commit, at-most-once application and fresh progress after recovery.

The original liveness test comments out its final progress assertion. An initial stronger experiment requiring all nodes to apply within 10000ms failed in both C/C++; for example n=3/pre_vote ON/seed=85213 had two counts=129 and a lagger at 118. This is not treated as an original completion contract. Final checks require a successful fresh command and matching application by a quorum after disruption ends. None of the twelve selected schedules observed progress during disruption; this does not claim availability during disruption or bounded all-follower catch-up. Preserve that earlier full-convergence failure as a separate limit.

Additional reference limitation found by actual comparison: recv_request_vote_result.c reads candidate_state.in_pre_vote before checking role. On current x86-64 its bool offset=185 while follower.current_leader.address occupies offset=184/size=8, so followers read the second byte of their leader pointer. This is not meaningful retained election-phase state. Discarded the initial interpretation and did not copy inactive union bytes into fake protocol state outside the C++ variant. Initial unguarded C executions are diagnostic records, not reusable completion evidence. The final adapter retains each C io start/receive callback and marks comparison unavailable before handing a non-candidate VoteResult to the original handler. Exclude that event, close the C fixture and continue only C++ execution. These 133 stops do not verify the affected C branch or all C behavior.

A small independent regression also confirmed C++ follower/leader term persistence incorrectly treating a delayed version>=2/pre_vote=true reply's proposed term as actual. Before the fix, shared Release core_stale_vote failed with before=2/after=3. Updated receive_core.cpp to ignore this reply when not a candidate. Active-candidate filtering/tallying and actual-vote/version-1 higher-term updates remain unchanged. The 24 cases combine initial follower/former-candidate follower/single-voter leader×version 1/2×pre_vote false/true×lower/higher term. Verify term/vote/state/persistence flags/log-commit-message invariants without using the inactive C union branch as oracle. Added no union-mirroring state field or public signature.

These are neither remaining feature counts nor a porting percentage. Do not declare the whole library complete. References cover the original tests/sources above, C++ buffer/entry/log/trail/configuration/error/random/compression/byte/memory allocators, comparisons and cleanup. Product implementation is unchanged. New files are tests/foundation_failure.cpp and docs/ko-en/configuration.md; updated test CMake, links from existing core/node APIs and these records. Preserve reference raft/.

New foundation_failure is a C++ public/internal-value consumer without the C reference library. Four long-address members exercise standard string/vector allocations. Inject one failure at a time for configuration add/copy/encode/decode, log append/growing append/configuration append and ordinary/resource-vector acquire. Stop each operation at first success rather than repeating already-successful positions. Check input/existing output/log prefix/old owning handles on failure and retry without stale busy keys. Ordinary/31-entry prefixes also cover rollback after reference registration when storage expands.

| Operation | Observed failing positions in this tool environment |
| --- | --- |
| configuration.add / copy / encode / decode | 2 / 5 / 1 / 7 |
| log.append / growing_append / append_configuration | 2 / 3 / 4 |
| log.acquire / acquire_resource | 1 / 1 |
| Total | 26 |

Four remove cases cover first/middle/last/unknown IDs without consuming fail-next, preserving order/errors. Four truncate cases following failure/success of both acquire overloads likewise retain acquired values without allocation. The same term/index remains busy until the last owning handle is released, then becomes reusable. Do not artificially reproduce C remove's new-array allocation or C log reference-table allocation faults in standard containers. Distinguish standard copy-construction exceptions from noexcept-method error codes; checks execute in Release.

The initial new check passed three configurations. Added original fixed random vectors and storage-expansion rollback during the actual audit, so only the changed new check was rebuilt/rerun. Preserve initial -initial.log/-golden.log and initial/final/growth build logs separately from final results. Product/existing tests were unchanged, so did not rerun the existing full suite.

This contract correspondence audit removes these fifteen files from the pending case inventory. Remaining original case auditing consists of three native unit files/40 definitions and thirteen native integration files/190 definitions, totaling sixteen files/230 definitions. The table maps functional contracts to evidence; it does not assert identical original trace strings or every preprocessor/parameter combination. Previously recorded reference/environmental limitations and whole-scope completion proof remain separate outstanding work. Product/public signatures/options/install/exports/dependencies and the original raft/ tree are unchanged. Actual edits are four new functions/dispatch branches in tests/core.cpp, four registrations/timeout on the same core target in tests/CMakeLists.txt, paired core APIs, and this progress record.

The four new checks total thirty cases/123 C event comparisons per configuration. The existing comparator checks start/step/queries, state variant, term/vote, configurations/indices, trail/cache, timeout, message fields, persistence/snapshot outputs, and bytes. Retained snapshot and previous-write batches in snapshot_interleaving keep their bytes through subsequent steps; the retired header expires at final-handle release. Do not manufacture a C comparison by reporting discarded write completions outside the original core's preconditions.

The initial shared Release snapshot retry check failed because consecutive timeouts without heartbeat replies made its peer unresponsive. C and C++ comparators matched up to that point. Setting heartbeat=50ms and receiving a valid reply at 82ms before the 102ms snapshot deadline establishes the original retry condition; only that check was rerun and passed. Reuse the catch-up pass from the same initial run. The first extended restore/replication build failed because configuration has no operator==; after replacing it with server-field comparison, build exit 0 was confirmed before the first runtime. Both failure logs are preserved; no old binary was used for new verification.

The finite original inventory of 43 files/494 literal TEST definitions is now mapped through fuzzy 4/12, core-unit 8/134, core/node integration 15/118, and native 16/230 correspondence. This is not a feature percentage, all-Munit-parameter pass, or proof of every execution path. Instead of continually adding unmapped source files, next compare a completion decision matrix against the full requirements/current source/public APIs/interfaces/options/ownership/validation limits. Environmentally unavailable real-device/kernel checks, original C undefined behavior, initial TCP timeout, and convergence limits remain explicit; the whole goal is not marked complete.

Product/public signatures/dependencies/options/install/exports and original raft/ sources are unchanged. Actual edits are a new load function in tests/segments.cpp, persistence.cpp dispatch, standalone resolver.cpp/target, a combined check/dispatch in uv_io.cpp, a new C bridge in log_writer_reference.c, and three CTest registrations. Existing functions/shared-helper behavior are retained. Paired uv_io/TCP APIs and this progress record are updated together.

New storage_load_contracts copies identical input files into separate directories and compares status, term/vote/start index, all entry/snapshot metadata/payloads, failure-output preservation, cleanup/quarantine filenames, and every non-quarantined file byte. Corrupt-file timestamps are normalized; the existing compare_load helper is unchanged. First batches truncated at 4/16/32/44 bytes return io_error with auto-recovery ON/OFF; middle closed corruption is not discarded by automatic recovery.

The standalone resolver executable injects getaddrinfo/freeaddrinfo only for raftpp.test and forwards other calls to the original system functions. Synchronous listeners and libuv workers share a mutex/allocation registry, used by both actual C/C++ implementations. Four connection/five listener outcomes, callback counts, identities/addresses, and handshake bytes match. Final Debug/Release/ASan runs free fourteen/fourteen/fifteen resolver results respectively and empty the registry. Canceled workers may stop before resolver dispatch. All actual connections are loopback; the nonlocal address is used only for failing bind. Injection is linked into neither the existing tcp_failure executable nor product. The initial check called set_bind_address after init and failed with busy; initializing the desired address directly corrected the precondition, then only the new check was rerun. This is not recorded as a product defect.

Final source inspection found that UvTcpListenOnMultipleIP in raft/src/uv_tcp_listen.c calls raft_free on the listener array immediately after uv_close(..., NULL) on error. Native handle storage must survive close completion, so the initial default-heap failed-bind paths are excluded as reference lifetime-safety evidence. Only the two C first/second bind-failure cases now use deferred_heap through the public raft_heap_get/set API: delegate to the original allocator, retain free requests, and actually free/restore the original allocator after that loop's close/drain/uv_loop_close. C requests/results/callbacks/bind logic are unchanged. The final nine comparisons therefore comprise seven default-heap and two deferred-free-heap C cases; this does not claim the original early free fixed. -resolver-<profile>-deferred-build.log/-deferred.log are final inputs/results, distinguished from earlier default-heap logs. Existing C++ standard owners/native close callbacks provide the required lifetime.

The original duplicate workaround applies only to exactly two addresses, and traversal of three or more addresses also returns to the second node each time. C++ traverses the actual list and skips every identical IP/port pair. New runtime comparisons cover two candidates/listeners; they do not claim fresh comparison of the original triple-duplicate case or every resolver-list length.

New file_snapshot_truncate executes the actual native C snapshot/truncate/barrier/append paths through a separate helper. C requests/snapshot/entry payloads remain alive through close/drain. Since C truncate has no completion callback, subsequent actual-write completion establishes its finish. Existing C helper bodies/original sources are unchanged.
